 K_{X}\simeq\pi^{*}\mathscr{O}(2). KX≃π∗​𝒪​(2).K_{X}\simeq\pi^{*}\mathscr{O}(2).

(3.1)


It is not hard to show that one can construct such bundles over the base  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} (see, e.g., the suggestion by D. Maulik in [15] for one example).
Then we can build a kind of twistor space





 \mathbb{PT}_{X}=\pi^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1))\rightarrow X. ℙ​𝕋X=π∗​(𝒪⁡(1)⊕𝒪⁡(1))→X.\mathbb{PT}_{X}=\pi^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1))\rightarrow X.

(3.2)


As a smooth manifold,  \mathbb{PT}_{X}=X\times\mathbb{R}^{4} ℙ​𝕋X=X×ℝ4\mathbb{PT}_{X}=X\times\mathbb{R}^{4}. This fibers (holomorphically) over the ordinary twistor space  \mathbb{PT}=\mathbb{CP}^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{4} ℙ​𝕋=ℂ​ℙ1×ℝ4\mathbb{PT}=\mathbb{CP}^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}, and the fibers are elliptic K3 surfaces. Points in complexified spacetime are  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1}’s in ordinary twistor space. In our modified twistor space  \mathbb{PT}_{X} ℙ​𝕋X\mathbb{PT}_{X}, points in complexified spacetime are copies of  X XX inside  \mathbb{PT}_{X} ℙ​𝕋X\mathbb{PT}_{X}. Because the normal bundle to  X XX in  \mathbb{PT}_{X} ℙ​𝕋X\mathbb{PT}_{X} is  \pi^{\ast}\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\pi^{\ast}\mathscr{O}(1) π∗​𝒪​(1)⊕π∗​𝒪​(1)\pi^{\ast}\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\pi^{\ast}\mathscr{O}(1), there is a four-dimensional moduli space of such.




Our model is given by the type I string compactified on  \mathbb{PT}_{X} ℙ​𝕋X\mathbb{PT}_{X}. As usual, we must include 32 space-filling D9 branes plus an O9 plane. In flat space, the D9 branes contribute an  \mathrm{SO}(32) SO⁡(32)\mathrm{SO}(32) gauge group, though in our curved geometry we must turn on nontrivial gauge fluxes on the D9 worldvolume to cancel the resulting anomaly. At a generic point in the vector bundle moduli space, this breaks the gauge group to  \mathrm{SO}(8) SO⁡(8)\mathrm{SO}(8). (This is perhaps most easily seen by S-dualizing to the  \mathrm{SO}(32) SO⁡(32)\mathrm{SO}(32) heterotic string. When compactifying the heterotic string on a Calabi-Yau, one must turn on vector bundle moduli corresponding to flat gauge bundles. Such a vector bundle at a generic point in the moduli space of a K3 fibration will break the gauge group from  \mathrm{SO}(32)\rightarrow\mathrm{SO}(8) SO⁡(32)→SO⁡(8)\mathrm{SO}(32)\rightarrow\mathrm{SO}(8), i.e. has second Chern class equal to 16 [69]). For tadpole cancellation on a compact geometry, one must also wrap a prescribed number of D5 branes on  X XX. We will elide the details of this computation by considering a decompactification limit of our geometry, so that we may add an arbitrary number  N NN of D5s on  X XX. In this limit, the  \mathrm{SO}(8) SO⁡(8)\mathrm{SO}(8) gauge group becomes a flavor symmetry group as its coupling goes to zero, presaging the role it will play in our chiral algebra.




From this basic model, one can consider a variety of duality frames. We will content ourselves with making contact with the IIB frame sketched in [15], though it is interesting to note than an explicit F-theory uplift of this geometry, with non-Higgsable SO(8) gauge group, can be constructed 1010
              10
              
              
              
            We are grateful to M. Kim for very useful discussions on this..




First, we may consider four T-dualities along the K3 fiber of  X XX (for example, we may consider the Kummer, or  T^{4}/\mathbb{Z}_{2} T4/ℤ2T^{4}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}, locus in the K3 moduli space to explicitly perform this operation). This produces the type  \text{I}^{\prime} I′\text{I}^{\prime} string on  \mathbb{PT}_{X} ℙ​𝕋X\mathbb{PT}_{X}, where the D9/O9 system has become a D5/O5 system wrapping the twistor space of  \mathbb{R}^{4} ℝ4\mathbb{R}^{4}, namely the directions  \mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1)\rightarrow\mathbb{CP}^{1} 𝒪⁡(1)⊕𝒪⁡(1)→ℂ​ℙ1\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1)\rightarrow\mathbb{CP}^{1}, and the D5 branes have become  N NN D1 branes on the base  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1}. Notice that since the D1 and D5 branes are parallel, this is a 1/4-BPS system, preserving 8 supercharges.




An additional two T-dualities can be performed on the elliptic fiber directions of the K3 fiber to reach a frame with a D7/O7 system and  N NN D3 branes. The type I string on the  \mathbb{PT}_{X} ℙ​𝕋X\mathbb{PT}_{X} geometry is equivalent, as always, to an orientifold of the IIB string. From the IIB perspective, we have a D7/O7 system hosting an SO(8) gauge group, with  N NN D3 branes hosting an  \mathrm{Sp}(N) Sp⁡(N)\mathrm{Sp}(N) gauge group.1111
              11
              
              
              
            The geometric part of the  \mathbb{Z}_{2} ℤ2\mathbb{Z}_{2} involution can be taken to act naturally on the elliptic fiber of  \mathbb{PT}_{X} ℙ​𝕋X\mathbb{PT}_{X}, yielding a  T^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\simeq\mathbb{CP}^{1} T2/ℤ2≃ℂ​ℙ1T^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\simeq\mathbb{CP}^{1} fibered over  \mathbb{CP}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1→ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{CP}^{1}.
To this 10d duality frame, we may turn on an Omega-background along the (decompactification limit of the) four fiber directions (i.e. the K3 fiber directions in the  \mathbb{PT}_{X} ℙ​𝕋X\mathbb{PT}_{X} geometry). This procedure results in an effective compactification down to the 6 twistor space directions, and an effective Euclidean D1/D5/O5- system. From this IIB frame one may pass to the topological string B-model “subsector”, or equivalently the type I topological string.
It is this 6d type I topological string theory on  \mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1)\rightarrow\mathbb{CP}^{1} 𝒪⁡(1)⊕𝒪⁡(1)→ℂ​ℙ1\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1)\rightarrow\mathbb{CP}^{1}, with noncompact D5 branes supporting an  \mathrm{SO}(8) SO⁡(8)\mathrm{SO}(8) flavor symmetry and compact D1 branes supporting an  \mathrm{Sp}(N) Sp⁡(N)\mathrm{Sp}(N) gauge symmetry, that we will study in the remainder of this work.





3.2 The backreacted twistor geometry




For the rest of this section, we revert to working in the simpler setting of topological strings. Let us start with  \mathbb{PT}=\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1)\to\mathbb{CP}^{1} ℙ​𝕋=𝒪⁡(1)⊕𝒪⁡(1)→ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{PT}=\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1)\to\mathbb{CP}^{1} and wrap  N\geq 0 N≥0N\geq 0 D1 branes along the zero section  v^{\dot{\alpha}}=0 vα˙=0v^{\dot{\alpha}}=0 (see section 2.1 for a review of our notation). This zero section is the twistor line  L_{0} L0L_{0} corresponding to the origin of  \mathbb{R}^{4} ℝ4\mathbb{R}^{4}. We study the brane backreaction by adding a source term to the equation of motion (2.33) of BCOV theory on  \mathbb{PT} ℙ​𝕋\mathbb{PT}.




Following [16], the bulk-brane coupling adds a source term





 \frac{N}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{L_{0}}\partial^{-1}(\mu\,\lrcorner\,\Omega)\,,\qquad\Omega=\frac{\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{z^{2}}\,, N2​π​i​∫L0∂−1(μ​⌟​Ω),Ω=d​z​d2​vz2,\frac{N}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{L_{0}}\partial^{-1}(\mu\,\lrcorner\,\Omega)\,,\qquad\Omega=\frac{\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{z^{2}}\,,

(3.3)


to the action of BCOV theory. As a result, the D1 branes backreact to generate a nontrivial complex structure deformation  \bar{\partial}\mapsto\bar{\partial}+\mu ∂¯↦∂¯+μ\bar{\partial}\mapsto\bar{\partial}+\mu determined by the Maurer-Cartan equation with a delta function source





 \bar{\partial}\mu+\frac{1}{2}\,[\mu,\mu]=N\,(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}\,\bar{\delta}^{2}(v)\,z^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\,. ∂¯​μ+12​[μ,μ]=N​(2​π​i)2​δ¯2​(v)​z2​∂∂z.\bar{\partial}\mu+\frac{1}{2}\,[\mu,\mu]=N\,(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}\,\bar{\delta}^{2}(v)\,z^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\,.

(3.4)


Here,  \bar{\partial}=\mathrm{d}\bar{z}\,\partial_{\bar{z}}+\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{\alpha}\,\partial_{\bar{v}^{\alpha}}=\mathrm{d}\bar{z}\,\partial_{\bar{z}}+\mathrm{d}\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,\partial_{\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}} ∂¯=dz¯∂z¯+dv¯α∂v¯α=dz¯∂z¯+dv^α˙∂v^α˙\bar{\partial}=\mathrm{d}\bar{z}\,\partial_{\bar{z}}+\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{\alpha}\,\partial_{\bar{v}^{\alpha}}=\mathrm{d}\bar{z}\,\partial_{\bar{z}}+\mathrm{d}\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,\partial_{\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}} is the antiholomorphic exterior derivative on  \mathbb{PT} ℙ​𝕋\mathbb{PT}, and  \bar{\delta}^{2}(v)=\delta(v^{\dot{1}})\delta(v^{\dot{2}})\,\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{1}\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{2} δ¯2​(v)=δ⁡(v1˙)​δ​(v2˙)​d​v¯1∧d​v¯2\bar{\delta}^{2}(v)=\delta(v^{\dot{1}})\delta(v^{\dot{2}})\,\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{1}\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{2} denotes the complex delta distribution with support at  v^{\dot{\alpha}}=0 vα˙=0v^{\dot{\alpha}}=0. The factor of  z^{2} z2z^{2} on the right implies that the source vanishes at  z=0,\infty z=0,∞z=0,\infty. It ensures consistency with the boundary conditions that  \mu μ\mu (and hence the left hand side) vanishes to second order at  z=0,\infty z=0,∞z=0,\infty each. In particular, replacing  z\mapsto 1/z z↦1/zz\mapsto 1/z maps  z^{2}\partial_{z}\mapsto-\partial_{z} z2∂z↦−∂zz^{2}\partial_{z}\mapsto-\partial_{z}, showing that  1/z 1/z1/z acts like a more natural coordinate on our D1 brane worldvolume. We will occasionally return to this observation in later sections. It was mainly used in [36] to simplify the expressions of certain scattering amplitudes.




The solution for the Beltrami differential that we are looking for is given by





 \mu=-N\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]}{\|v\|^{4}}\,z^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\,. μ=−N​[v^​d​v^]‖v‖4​z2​∂∂z.\mu=-N\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]}{\|v\|^{4}}\,z^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\,.

(3.5)

Since  [\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]=\overline{[v\,\mathrm{d}v]}=\bar{v}^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{1}-\bar{v}^{1}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{2} [v^​d​v^]=[v​d​v]¯=v¯2​d​v¯1−v¯1​d​v¯2[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]=\overline{[v\,\mathrm{d}v]}=\bar{v}^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{1}-\bar{v}^{1}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}^{2}, we observe that  -[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]/\|v\|^{4} −[v^dv^]/∥v∥4-[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]/\|v\|^{4} is the Bochner-Martinelli kernel of the dbar operator on each  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2} fiber at fixed  z zz. Hence,





 \bar{\partial}\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]}{\|v\|^{4}}=\bar{\partial}|_{z}\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]}{\|v\|^{4}}=-(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}\,\bar{\delta}^{2}(v)\,. ∂¯​[v^​d​v^]‖v‖4=∂¯|z​[v^​d​v^]‖v‖4=−(2​π​i)2​δ¯2​(v).\bar{\partial}\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]}{\|v\|^{4}}=\bar{\partial}|_{z}\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]}{\|v\|^{4}}=-(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}\,\bar{\delta}^{2}(v)\,.

(3.6)


At the same time,  [\mu,\mu]\propto[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]\wedge[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]=0 [μ,μ]∝[v^​d​v^]∧[v^​d​v^]=0[\mu,\mu]\propto[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]\wedge[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]=0. So (3.5) solves (3.4).  \mu μ\mu is also divergence-free with respect to the holomorphic volume form  \mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}^{2}v/z^{2} d​z​d2​v/z2\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}^{2}v/z^{2}, i.e., it satisfies the constraint (2.35). This is checked in two steps:






 \displaystyle\eta=\mu\,\lrcorner\,\frac{\mathrm{d}z\wedge\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{z^{2}}=N\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]\wedge\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{\|v\|^{4}} η=μ​⌟​d​z∧d2​vz2=N​[v^​d​v^]∧d2​v‖v‖4\displaystyle\eta=\mu\,\lrcorner\,\frac{\mathrm{d}z\wedge\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{z^{2}}=N\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]\wedge\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{\|v\|^{4}}

 \displaystyle\implies ⟹\displaystyle\implies
 \displaystyle\partial\eta=2N\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]\wedge[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}v]\wedge\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{\|v\|^{6}}=0\,. ∂η=2​N​[v^​d​v^]∧[v^​d​v]∧d2​v‖v‖6=0.\displaystyle\partial\eta=2N\,\frac{[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}]\wedge[\hat{v}\,\mathrm{d}v]\wedge\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{\|v\|^{6}}=0\,.

(3.8)


Our derivation of the solution for  \mu μ\mu is completely analogous to the backreaction computed starting from the resolved conifold  \mathscr{O}(-1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(-1) 𝒪⁡(−1)⊕𝒪⁡(−1)\mathscr{O}(-1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(-1) in [16], up to the factor of  z^{2} z2z^{2} that comes from starting with  \mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1) 𝒪⁡(1)⊕𝒪⁡(1)\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1) instead.




Define the set





 Z_{N}\vcentcolon=\mathbb{PT}-\{v^{\dot{\alpha}}=0\} ZN:=ℙ𝕋−{vα˙=0}Z_{N}\vcentcolon=\mathbb{PT}-\{v^{\dot{\alpha}}=0\}

(3.9)


and equip it with the deformed complex structure  \bar{\partial}+\mu ∂¯+μ\bar{\partial}+\mu. We will refer to  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} with this complex structure as our backreacted geometry, with the convention that  Z_{0} Z0Z_{0} corresponds to zero backreaction.  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} can again be covered by the two patches  z\neq 0 z≠0z\neq 0 and  z\neq\infty z≠∞z\neq\infty, so let us work in the patch  z\neq 0 z≠0z\neq 0.




A smooth function  f(z,v) f⁡(z,v)f(z,v) is holomorphic in the deformed complex structure if and only if  (\bar{\partial}+\mu)f=0 (∂¯+μ)​f=0(\bar{\partial}+\mu)f=0. Equivalently, it is holomorphic if it is annihilated by the deformed  (0,1) (0,1)(0,1)-vector fields





 \frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}\,,\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}}+\frac{N\hat{v}_{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|v\|^{4}}\,z^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\,. ∂∂z¯,∂∂v^α˙+N​v^α˙‖v‖4​z2​∂∂z.\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}\,,\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}}+\frac{N\hat{v}_{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|v\|^{4}}\,z^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\,.

(3.10)


These annihilate  v^{\dot{\alpha}} vα˙v^{\dot{\alpha}} as well as the combinations





 w^{\dot{\alpha}}=\frac{v^{\dot{\alpha}}}{z}-\frac{N\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|v\|^{2}}\,, wα˙=vα˙z−N​v^α˙‖v‖2,w^{\dot{\alpha}}=\frac{v^{\dot{\alpha}}}{z}-\frac{N\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|v\|^{2}}\,,

(3.11)


so that any three out of the four quantities  v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}} vα˙,wα˙v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}} can provide holomorphic coordinates on the patch  \{z\neq 0\}\subset Z_{N} {z≠0}⊂ZN\{z\neq 0\}\subset Z_{N}. Contracting (3.11) with  \hat{v}_{\dot{\alpha}} v^α˙\hat{v}_{\dot{\alpha}} and using the relation  [\hat{v}\,v]=\|v\|^{2} [v^​v]=‖v‖2[\hat{v}\,v]=\|v\|^{2}, we can also invert (3.11) to solve for  z zz,





 z=\frac{\|v\|^{2}}{[\hat{v}\,w]}\,. z=‖v‖2[v^​w].z=\frac{\|v\|^{2}}{[\hat{v}\,w]}\,.

(3.12)


This is no longer a holomorphic coordinate as it depends non-holomorphically on  v^{\dot{\alpha}} vα˙v^{\dot{\alpha}}. A similar analysis can also be performed in the “antipodal patch”  \{z\neq\infty\} {z≠∞}\{z\neq\infty\}, but we will provide a more global description shortly.




Since  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} has three complex dimensions, the four holomorphic quantities  v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}} vα˙,wα˙v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}} must be interrelated. Indeed, they are easily seen to satisfy





 [v\,w]\equiv v^{\dot{2}}w^{\dot{1}}-v^{\dot{1}}w^{\dot{2}}=N\,. [v​w]≡v2˙​w1˙−v1˙​w2˙=N.[v\,w]\equiv v^{\dot{2}}w^{\dot{1}}-v^{\dot{1}}w^{\dot{2}}=N\,.

(3.13)


Whenever  N\neq 0 N≠0N\neq 0, we can rewrite this as the condition





 \frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\begin{pmatrix}w^{\dot{1}}&&v^{\dot{1}}\\
w^{\dot{2}}&&v^{\dot{2}}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,, 1N​(w1˙v1˙w2˙v2˙)∈SL2​(ℂ),\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\begin{pmatrix}w^{\dot{1}}&&v^{\dot{1}}\\
w^{\dot{2}}&&v^{\dot{2}}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,,

(3.14)


i.e., that this  2\times 2 2×22\times 2 matrix has unit determinant. Therefore, the map  (v^{\dot{\alpha}},z)\mapsto(v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}}) (vα˙,z)↦(vα˙,wα˙)(v^{\dot{\alpha}},z)\mapsto(v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}}) identifies this patch of  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} with the group manifold of  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}). The complex structure of  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} is then simply the standard complex structure of  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}).




This mechanism of obtaining  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} from  \mathbb{PT} ℙ​𝕋\mathbb{PT} by brane backreaction is a twistorial analogue of the twisted holographic backreaction computed in [16, 17], and is also redolent of the geometric transition from the resolved conifold to the deformed conifold.



Deformed twistor lines.




We wish to interpret  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} as the twistor space of some asymptotically flat spacetime. To do this, we need to find a 4-parameter family of rational curves that are holomorphic with respect to  \bar{\partial}+\mu ∂¯+μ\bar{\partial}+\mu and have normal bundle  \mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1) 𝒪⁡(1)⊕𝒪⁡(1)\mathscr{O}(1)\oplus\mathscr{O}(1). They are also required to be invariant under a suitable real structure on  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N}. These will act as our deformed twistor lines.




Let us first quote the result. In the coordinates  v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}} vα˙,wα˙v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}}, the deformed twistor lines are captured by the new incidence relations





 v^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=u^{\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,,\qquad w^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\frac{u^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\sigma}\left(1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\right)\,, vα˙​(σ)=uα˙+σ​u^α˙,wα˙​(σ)=u^α˙+uα˙σ​(1+N‖u‖2),v^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=u^{\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,,\qquad w^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\frac{u^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\sigma}\left(1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\right)\,,

(3.15)


where  \sigma σ\sigma is a stereographic coordinate on  \mathbb{CP}^{1}\simeq S^{2} ℂ​ℙ1≃S2\mathbb{CP}^{1}\simeq S^{2}. These satisfy  [v(\sigma)\,w(\sigma)]=N [v⁡(σ)​w​(σ)]=N[v(\sigma)\,w(\sigma)]=N and are parametrized by the four real moduli contained in  u^{\dot{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 uα˙∈ℂ2−0u^{\dot{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}-0. As before,  \hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}=(-\bar{u}^{2},\bar{u}^{1}) u^α˙=(−u¯2,u¯1)\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}=(-\bar{u}^{2},\bar{u}^{1}). Along such a curve, the coordinate  z zz varies non-holomorphically:





 z(\sigma)=\sigma-\frac{N\sigma}{N+(1+|\sigma|^{2})\|u\|^{2}}\,. z⁡(σ)=σ−N​σN+(1+|σ|2)​‖u‖2.z(\sigma)=\sigma-\frac{N\sigma}{N+(1+|\sigma|^{2})\|u\|^{2}}\,.

(3.16)


As  N\to 0 N→0N\to 0, we get  z(\sigma)\to\sigma z⁡(σ)→σz(\sigma)\to\sigma and the curves revert to the twistor lines of  \mathbb{PT} ℙ​𝕋\mathbb{PT}.




The twistor lines (3.15) are defined as written for  u\neq 0 u≠0u\neq 0. However, they do make sense in the  \left\|u\right\|\to 0 ‖u‖→0\left\|u\right\|\to 0 limit. If we send  \left\|u\right\|\to 0 ‖u‖→0\left\|u\right\|\to 0 while making the reparametrization  \sigma\mapsto\frac{\sigma}{\left\|u\right\|} σ↦σ‖u‖\sigma\mapsto\frac{\sigma}{\left\|u\right\|}, the twistor line (3.15) becomes





 v^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|u\|}\,,\qquad w^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\frac{Nu^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\sigma\|u\|}\,, vα˙​(σ)=σ​u^α˙‖u‖,wα˙​(σ)=N​uα˙σ​‖u‖,v^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|u\|}\,,\qquad w^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\frac{Nu^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\sigma\|u\|}\,,

(3.17)


which is well-defined in the  \|u\|\to 0 ‖u‖→0\|u\|\to 0 limit. This twistor line depends only on the phase of  u/\|u\| u/‖u‖u/\|u\|, but a change of phase can also be absorbed into a reparametrization of  \sigma σ\sigma. Therefore it only depends on the projectivization of the 2-spinor  u^{\dot{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 uα˙∈ℂ2−0u^{\dot{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}-0.




What we have learned is that twistor lines (3.15) are associated, not to points in  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2}, but to points in its blow-up  \widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} ℂ~2\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} at the origin. In other words, the backreaction on twistor space has changed the topology of spacetime by blowing up its origin. We will see this again from the perspective of the spacetime metric shortly.




More generally, wrapping D1 branes along the twistor line of a point  x\in\mathbb{C}^{2} x∈ℂ2x\in\mathbb{C}^{2} will induce a geometric transition that blows up  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2} at  x xx. Hence, our analysis concretely realizes the expectations outlined in the prescient work of Hartnoll and Policastro [41].




Being patchwise diffeomorphic to  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}), the deformed geometry  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} comes equipped with a natural real structure induced by the antiholomorphic map





 (v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}})\mapsto(-\hat{w}^{\dot{\alpha}},\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}})\,. (vα˙,wα˙)↦(−w^α˙,v^α˙).(v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}})\mapsto(-\hat{w}^{\dot{\alpha}},\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}})\,.

(3.18)


Due to the property  \hat{\hat{v}}^{\dot{\alpha}}=-v^{\dot{\alpha}},\hat{\hat{w}}^{\dot{\alpha}}=-w^{\dot{\alpha}} v^^α˙=−vα˙,w^^α˙=−wα˙\hat{\hat{v}}^{\dot{\alpha}}=-v^{\dot{\alpha}},\hat{\hat{w}}^{\dot{\alpha}}=-w^{\dot{\alpha}}, this map is an involution on  \mathbb{C}^{4} ℂ4\mathbb{C}^{4}. Complex conjugation shows that  v^{\dot{\alpha}}w_{\dot{\alpha}}=N\iff(-\hat{w}^{\dot{\alpha}})\hat{v}_{\dot{\alpha}}=N vα˙​wα˙=N⇔(−w^α˙)​v^α˙=Nv^{\dot{\alpha}}w_{\dot{\alpha}}=N\iff(-\hat{w}^{\dot{\alpha}})\hat{v}_{\dot{\alpha}}=N, so it descends to a well-defined involution on  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}). The set of fixed points of (3.18) would have been  \{w^{\dot{\alpha}}=\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}\} {wα˙=v^α˙}\{w^{\dot{\alpha}}=\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}\}, but such points satisfy  [v\,w]=[v\,\hat{v}]=-\|v\|^{2}<0 [v​w]=[v​v^]=−‖v‖2<0[v\,w]=[v\,\hat{v}]=-\|v\|^{2}<0. Since  N>0 N>0N>0, they cannot satisfy  [v\,w]=N [v​w]=N[v\,w]=N, so this subset of  \mathbb{C}^{4} ℂ4\mathbb{C}^{4} does not intersect  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}). Hence, our involution is fixed-point-free.




Crucially, our curves (3.15) are invariant under this involution. This is best seen by reparametrizing the curves through a change of coordinate





 \sigma\mapsto\sigma\,\sqrt{1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}}\,. σ↦σ​1+N‖u‖2.\sigma\mapsto\sigma\,\sqrt{1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}}\,.

(3.19)


The equations of the curve now read1212

 v^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=u^{\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}\sqrt{1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}}\,,\qquad w^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\frac{u^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\sigma}\sqrt{1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}}\,. vα˙​(σ)=uα˙+σ​u^α˙​1+N‖u‖2,wα˙​(σ)=u^α˙+uα˙σ​1+N‖u‖2.v^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=u^{\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}\sqrt{1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}}\,,\qquad w^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\frac{u^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\sigma}\sqrt{1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}}\,.

(3.20)


In this parametrization, we observe that if our curve passes through  (v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}}) (vα˙,wα˙)(v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}}) at a given point  \sigma=\sigma_{0} σ=σ0\sigma=\sigma_{0}, then it also passes through  (-\hat{w}^{\dot{\alpha}},\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}) (−w^α˙,v^α˙)(-\hat{w}^{\dot{\alpha}},\hat{v}^{\dot{\alpha}}) at the antipodal point  \sigma=-1/\bar{\sigma}_{0} σ=−1/σ¯0\sigma=-1/\bar{\sigma}_{0}. So our curves are the required real twistor lines. For practical calculations however, it will be easiest to stick with the parametrization (3.15).




Actually, there are some further subtleties in this identification. Taken at face value, the curves in (3.15) map the poles  \sigma=0,\infty σ=0,∞\sigma=0,\infty to the  [v\,w]\to 0 [v​w]→0[v\,w]\to 0 boundary of  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}). To obtain a genuine twistor space, we need to enlarge  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} so as to also include such boundary points. This is resolved by the observation that  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} embeds into the twistor space of  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2}.




As we will see shortly, the backreaction on spacetime produces the Burns geometry, which is a manifold conformally equivalent to  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} with a point at  \infty ∞\infty removed (cf. the discussion around equation (3.53)). As a smooth manifold, the latter is diffeomorphic (in an orientation-reversing manner) to the blow-up of  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2} at the origin, which includes  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 as a coordinate patch. Since twistor space as a complex manifold (without any volume form) only depends on the conformal structure on the  4 44-manifold, the full twistor space of the Burns geometry is the same as that of  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2}, with a point at  \infty ∞\infty removed.




The twistor space of  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} equipped with its Fubini-Study metric is the flag variety  \mathbb{F} 𝔽\mathbb{F} of points in lines in  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} [47, 70]





 \mathbb{F}=\bigl\{([V^{k}],[W_{k}])\in\mathbb{CP}^{2}\times\mathbb{CP}^{2}\,\bigl|\,V^{k}W_{k}=0\bigr\}\,,\qquad k\in\{1,2,3\}\,. 𝔽={([Vk],[Wk])∈ℂ​ℙ2×ℂ​ℙ2|Vk​Wk=0},k∈{1,2,3}.\mathbb{F}=\bigl\{([V^{k}],[W_{k}])\in\mathbb{CP}^{2}\times\mathbb{CP}^{2}\,\bigl|\,V^{k}W_{k}=0\bigr\}\,,\qquad k\in\{1,2,3\}\,.

(3.21)


There exists a (non-holomorphic) twistor fibration  \mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{CP}^{2} 𝔽→ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{CP}^{2} whose projection map is given by a cross product of 3-vectors





 ([V],[W])\mapsto[\bar{V}\times W]\equiv[\epsilon^{klm}\bar{V}_{l}W_{m}] ([V],[W])↦[V¯×W]≡[ϵk​l​m​V¯l​Wm]([V],[W])\mapsto[\bar{V}\times W]\equiv[\epsilon^{klm}\bar{V}_{l}W_{m}]

(3.22)


with  \epsilon^{klm} ϵk​l​m\epsilon^{klm} the Levi-Civita symbol, and  \bar{V}_{l}\equiv\overline{V^{l}} V¯l≡Vl¯\bar{V}_{l}\equiv\overline{V^{l}} denoting componentwise complex conjugation. The fibers of this fibration are rationally embedded  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1}’s that get identified with twistor lines. Hence,  \mathbb{F} 𝔽\mathbb{F} is diffeomorphic to a sphere bundle over  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} as required.




 Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} embeds holomorphically into  \mathbb{F} 𝔽\mathbb{F} via the map





 (v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}})\mapsto[V^{k}]=[v^{\dot{\alpha}},-\sqrt{N}]\,,\;[W_{k}]=[w_{\dot{\alpha}},\sqrt{N}]\,. (vα˙,wα˙)↦[Vk]=[vα˙,−N],[Wk]=[wα˙,N].(v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}})\mapsto[V^{k}]=[v^{\dot{\alpha}},-\sqrt{N}]\,,\;[W_{k}]=[w_{\dot{\alpha}},\sqrt{N}]\,.

(3.23)


So,  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} is the locus in the flag variety where both  V^{3} V3V^{3} and  W_{3} W3W_{3} are non-zero. To obtain the full twistor space, we need to adjoin the locus where exactly one of  V^{3},W_{3} V3,W3V^{3},W_{3} is zero. This is the quadric  V^{3}W_{3}=0 V3​W3=0V^{3}W_{3}=0, except for the locus  V^{3}=W_{3}=0 V3=W3=0V^{3}=W_{3}=0 that forms the twistor line of the point at infinity  [0,0,1]\in\mathbb{CP}^{2} [0,0,1]∈ℂ​ℙ2[0,0,1]\in\mathbb{CP}^{2} (the twistor lines are described below in more detail). This will play the role of Pontecorvo’s quadric in the backreacted geometry. Because of  V^{k}W_{k}=0 Vk​Wk=0V^{k}W_{k}=0, this is the same as the quadric  V^{1}W_{1}+V^{2}W_{2}=v^{\dot{\alpha}}w_{\dot{\alpha}}=0 V1​W1+V2​W2=vα˙​wα˙=0V^{1}W_{1}+V^{2}W_{2}=v^{\dot{\alpha}}w_{\dot{\alpha}}=0.




The locus where  V^{3}=0 V3=0V^{3}=0 arises when the vector  v^{\dot{\alpha}} vα˙v^{\dot{\alpha}} tends to  \infty ∞\infty while  w^{\dot{\alpha}} wα˙w^{\dot{\alpha}} remains finite. By adjoining this locus, we include the  \sigma=\infty σ=∞\sigma=\infty point in the parametrized curve (3.15). Similarly, the locus  W_{3}=0 W3=0W_{3}=0 arises when  w^{\dot{\alpha}}\to\infty wα˙→∞w^{\dot{\alpha}}\to\infty so that  \sigma\to 0 σ→0\sigma\to 0 in (3.15).




The divisor  \{V^{3}=0,W_{3}\neq 0\} {V3=0,W3≠0}\{V^{3}=0,W_{3}\neq 0\} is a copy of the blow-up of  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2} at  0 00. To see this, we note that on this locus, we can set  W_{3}=1 W3=1W_{3}=1. Then the region is parameterized by  v^{\dot{\alpha}} vα˙v^{\dot{\alpha}} and  w^{\dot{\alpha}} wα˙w^{\dot{\alpha}} satisfying  [v\,w]=0 [v​w]=0[v\,w]=0, where  v vv is projective. If  w\neq 0 w≠0w\neq 0, then  v vv must be the projectivization of  w ww. If  w=0 w=0w=0, then  v vv is arbitrary; so we have inserted into the  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2} parametrized by  w ww a copy of  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} at  0 00. Similarly for the locus  \{W^{3}=0,V^{3}\neq 0\} {W3=0,V3≠0}\{W^{3}=0,V^{3}\neq 0\}.




Thus, the full twistor space we are interested in is obtained by adjoining to our backreacted space  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) two copies of the blow-up of  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2}. Every twistor line intersects each of these divisors in a point. We continue to refer to this completed twistor space as  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} by a mild abuse of notation.




The flag variety also includes the locus  V^{3}=W_{3}=0 V3=W3=0V^{3}=W_{3}=0. On this locus,  v,w v,wv,w are projective vectors and  [v\,w]=0 [v​w]=0[v\,w]=0. Thus,  v vv is proportional to  w ww, and this locus is a copy of  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1}. It is the diagonal  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} in  \mathbb{CP}^{1}_{v}\times\mathbb{CP}^{1}_{w} ℂ​ℙv1×ℂ​ℙw1\mathbb{CP}^{1}_{v}\times\mathbb{CP}^{1}_{w}. This  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} locus is not part of the twistor space of Burns space: it is the  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} corresponding to the point in  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} we have removed. It will be part of the asymptotic boundary.




We can now view our deformed incidence relations (3.15) as twistor lines of  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2}. To see this, let

(3.27)


This parametrization manifestly satisfies  V^{k}W_{k}=U^{k}W_{k}=V^{k}\bar{U}_{k}=0 Vk​Wk=Uk​Wk=Vk​U¯k=0V^{k}W_{k}=U^{k}W_{k}=V^{k}\bar{U}_{k}=0.




We can also show that these are real twistor lines of  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2}, i.e., they are preserved by the natural fixed-point-free antiholomorphic involution on  \mathbb{F} 𝔽\mathbb{F} that maps  ([V^{k}],[W_{k}])\mapsto([\bar{W}^{k}],[\bar{V}_{k}]) ([Vk],[Wk])↦([W¯k],[V¯k])([V^{k}],[W_{k}])\mapsto([\bar{W}^{k}],[\bar{V}_{k}]), where  \bar{V}_{k}\equiv\overline{V^{k}} V¯k≡Vk¯\bar{V}_{k}\equiv\overline{V^{k}},  \bar{W}^{k}\equiv\overline{W_{k}} W¯k≡Wk¯\bar{W}^{k}\equiv\overline{W_{k}}. To verify this, one computes





 [\bar{W}^{k}]=\left[\biggl(1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\biggr)\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}-\bar{\sigma}u^{\dot{\alpha}},\sqrt{N}\bar{\sigma}\right]\,,\quad[\bar{V}_{k}]=\left[-\hat{u}_{\dot{\alpha}}+\bar{\sigma}u_{\dot{\alpha}},-\sqrt{N}\right]\,, [W¯k]=[(1+N‖u‖2)​u^α˙−σ¯​uα˙,N​σ¯],[V¯k]=[−u^α˙+σ¯​uα˙,−N],[\bar{W}^{k}]=\left[\biggl(1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\biggr)\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}-\bar{\sigma}u^{\dot{\alpha}},\sqrt{N}\bar{\sigma}\right]\,,\quad[\bar{V}_{k}]=\left[-\hat{u}_{\dot{\alpha}}+\bar{\sigma}u_{\dot{\alpha}},-\sqrt{N}\right]\,,

(3.28)


from which one can derive that  \bar{W}^{k}\bar{V}_{k}=U^{k}\bar{V}_{k}=\bar{W}^{k}\bar{U}_{k}=0 W¯k​V¯k=Uk​V¯k=W¯k​U¯k=0\bar{W}^{k}\bar{V}_{k}=U^{k}\bar{V}_{k}=\bar{W}^{k}\bar{U}_{k}=0. So both  ([V^{k}],[W_{k}]) ([Vk],[Wk])([V^{k}],[W_{k}]) and its conjugate  ([\bar{W}^{k}],[\bar{V}_{k}]) ([W¯k],[V¯k])([\bar{W}^{k}],[\bar{V}_{k}]) lie on the same twistor line. We conclude that we have found the correct twistor lines required for constructing a self-dual spacetime with a metric of Euclidean signature. Since the twistor construction is conformally invariant, this metric will necessarily be conformal to the Fubini-Study metric.




In this picture, it is also clear that the twistor lines (3.15) correspond to points in the patch  \{U^{3}\neq 0\}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}-0\subset\mathbb{CP}^{2}-\{[0,0,1]\} {U3≠0}≃ℂ2−0⊂ℂℙ2−{[0,0,1]}\{U^{3}\neq 0\}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}-0\subset\mathbb{CP}^{2}-\{[0,0,1]\}. Points like  (v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}})=(\hat{\zeta}^{\dot{\alpha}}/\|\zeta\|,N\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}/\|\zeta\|) (vα˙,wα˙)=(ζ^α˙/‖ζ‖,N​ζα˙/‖ζ‖)(v^{\dot{\alpha}},w^{\dot{\alpha}})=(\hat{\zeta}^{\dot{\alpha}}/\|\zeta\|,N\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}/\|\zeta\|) also belong to the flag variety. But they project down to the set of points  [U^{k}]=[\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}},0] [Uk]=[ζα˙,0][U^{k}]=[\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}},0] in  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2}. Viewed as points on the blow-up of  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2} at  0 00, these are just the points on the exceptional divisor of the blow-up,  [\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{1} [ζα˙]∈ℂ​ℙ1[\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{1}. This is why they did not belong to any of our twistor lines (3.15). Instead, the appropriate twistor lines are found by setting  U^{k}=(\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}},0) Uk=(ζα˙,0)U^{k}=(\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}},0) in the unparametrized definition (3.26). We can also easily guess a parametrization for them as maps  \mathbb{CP}^{1}-\{0,\infty\}\to\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) ℂ​ℙ1−{0,∞}→SL2​(ℂ)\mathbb{CP}^{1}-\{0,\infty\}\to\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}),





 v^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma\hat{\zeta}^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|\zeta\|}\,,\qquad w^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\frac{N\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\sigma\|\zeta\|}\,. vα˙​(σ)=σ​ζ^α˙‖ζ‖,wα˙​(σ)=N​ζα˙σ​‖ζ‖.v^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma\hat{\zeta}^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|\zeta\|}\,,\qquad w^{\dot{\alpha}}(\sigma)=\frac{N\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\sigma\|\zeta\|}\,.

(3.29)


These agree with (3.17) if one sets  u^{\dot{\alpha}}=t\,\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}} uα˙=t​ζα˙u^{\dot{\alpha}}=t\,\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}} with  t\in\mathbb{C} t∈ℂt\in\mathbb{C},  [\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{1} [ζα˙]∈ℂ​ℙ1[\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{1}, and absorbs the phase of  t tt in a reparametrization of  \sigma σ\sigma. If needed, they may be written in a more symmetric fashion by rescaling  \sigma\mapsto\sigma\sqrt{N} σ↦σ​N\sigma\mapsto\sigma\sqrt{N}. Together with the lines (3.15), these foliate  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N}.




The backreacted spacetime geometry.




Finally, let us obtain the scalar-flat Kähler manifold that  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} corresponds to. We will derive the metric on  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} ℂ2−0⊂ℂ~2\mathbb{C}^{2}-0\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2}, and it will be shown to extend to the exceptional divisor of the blow-up in the next section.




The moduli space of the curves (3.15) is  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 and has complex coordinates  u^{\dot{\alpha}} uα˙u^{\dot{\alpha}}. Since  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} embeds into the twistor space of  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2}, the SD metric one obtains on  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 from the Penrose transform will be in the same conformal class as Fubini-Study (though with the opposite orientation). To fix a Kähler metric in this conformal class, we need to prescribe a meromorphic 3-form on  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N}.




There is a unique up to scale holomorphic volume form on  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) which is invariant under both the left and right actions of  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}). It is conveniently described by means of the Poincaré residue. Given an analytic hypersurface  f(z_{i})=0 f⁡(zi)=0f(z_{i})=0 embedded in  \mathbb{C}^{n} ℂn\mathbb{C}^{n} – with  z_{i} ziz_{i} the coordinates on  \mathbb{C}^{n} ℂn\mathbb{C}^{n} – a natural holomorphic volume form on it is given by the residue of  \mathrm{d}^{n}z/f dn​z/f\mathrm{d}^{n}z/f at  f=0 f=0f=0. For  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\subset Z_{N} SL2​(ℂ)⊂ZN\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\subset Z_{N}, a global  (3,0) (3,0)(3,0)-form is obtained by computing a residue of the volume form of  \mathbb{C}^{4} ℂ4\mathbb{C}^{4} along  \mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}) SL2​(ℂ)\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}),





 \Omega_{N}=-\text{Res}_{Z_{N}}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}v\wedge\mathrm{d}^{2}w}{[v\,w]-N}=-\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}v\wedge\mathrm{d}w^{\dot{1}}}{v^{\dot{1}}}\,, ΩN=−ResZN​d2​v∧d2​w[v​w]−N=−d2​v∧d​w1˙v1˙,\Omega_{N}=-\text{Res}_{Z_{N}}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}v\wedge\mathrm{d}^{2}w}{[v\,w]-N}=-\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}v\wedge\mathrm{d}w^{\dot{1}}}{v^{\dot{1}}}\,,

(3.30)


where the overall sign defines the orientation in which  \Omega_{N} ΩN\Omega_{N} reduces to  z^{-2}\mathrm{d}z\wedge\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{1}}\wedge\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{2}} z−2​d​z∧d​v1˙∧d​v2˙z^{-2}\mathrm{d}z\wedge\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{1}}\wedge\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{2}} as  N\to 0 N→0N\to 0 (see (3.32) below).




This can also be written as a weightless meromorphic 3-form on the embedding of  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N} in  \mathbb{F} 𝔽\mathbb{F},





 \Omega_{N}=\text{Res}_{\mathbb{F}}\,\frac{N}{(V^{3}W_{3})^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{D}^{2}V\wedge\mathrm{D}^{2}W}{V\cdot W}\,, ΩN=Res𝔽​N(V3​W3)2​D2​V∧D2​WV⋅W,\Omega_{N}=\text{Res}_{\mathbb{F}}\,\frac{N}{(V^{3}W_{3})^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{D}^{2}V\wedge\mathrm{D}^{2}W}{V\cdot W}\,,

(3.31)


with manifest second order poles on the quadric  V^{3}W_{3}=0 V3​W3=0V^{3}W_{3}=0. In this expression,  \mathrm{D}^{2}V=\frac{1}{2}\,\epsilon_{ijk}V^{i}\mathrm{d}V^{j}\wedge\mathrm{d}V^{k} D2​V=12​ϵi​j​k​Vi​d​Vj∧d​Vk\mathrm{D}^{2}V=\frac{1}{2}\,\epsilon_{ijk}V^{i}\mathrm{d}V^{j}\wedge\mathrm{d}V^{k} and  \mathrm{D}^{2}W=\frac{1}{2}\,\epsilon^{ijk}W_{i}\mathrm{d}W_{j}\wedge\mathrm{d}W_{j} D2​W=12​ϵi​j​k​Wi​d​Wj∧d​Wj\mathrm{D}^{2}W=\frac{1}{2}\,\epsilon^{ijk}W_{i}\mathrm{d}W_{j}\wedge\mathrm{d}W_{j} are weighted holomorphic top forms on the two copies of  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} involved in the definition (3.21), and the residue is taken at the hypersurface  V\cdot W=0 V⋅W=0V\cdot W=0 cutting out  \mathbb{F} 𝔽\mathbb{F}. Pulling back by the embedding (3.23), and using  V^{3}W_{3}=-(V^{1}W_{1}+V^{2}W_{2})=-v^{\dot{\alpha}}w_{\dot{\alpha}} V3​W3=−(V1​W1+V2​W2)=−vα˙​wα˙V^{3}W_{3}=-(V^{1}W_{1}+V^{2}W_{2})=-v^{\dot{\alpha}}w_{\dot{\alpha}} on  \mathbb{F} 𝔽\mathbb{F}, we see that (3.31) reduces to (3.30) away from the polar quadric  V^{3}W_{3}=0 V3​W3=0V^{3}W_{3}=0.




An important property of  \Omega_{N} ΩN\Omega_{N} is that its periods measure D1 brane charge [71, 25]. This is the B-model analogue of D branes carrying RR charges in type II strings. Indeed, suppose we rewrite  \Omega_{N} ΩN\Omega_{N} in the undeformed coordinates  z,v^{\dot{\alpha}} z,vα˙z,v^{\dot{\alpha}}. Substituting for  w^{\dot{1}} w1˙w^{\dot{1}} from (3.11), we can reexpress (3.30) as





 \Omega_{N}=\mathrm{d}^{2}v\wedge\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{z^{2}}+\frac{N\,\mathrm{D}\hat{v}}{\|v\|^{4}}\right)\,. ΩN=d2​v∧(d​zz2+N​D​v^‖v‖4).\Omega_{N}=\mathrm{d}^{2}v\wedge\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{z^{2}}+\frac{N\,\mathrm{D}\hat{v}}{\|v\|^{4}}\right)\,.

(3.32)


If we integrate this over any 3-cycle surrounding the brane locus  v^{\dot{\alpha}}=0 vα˙=0v^{\dot{\alpha}}=0, we find a multiple of the brane charge. For instance, integrating it over  \|v\|=1 ‖v‖=1\|v\|=1 at fixed  z zz yields





 \int_{z,\|v\|=1}\Omega=N\int_{\|v\|=1}\mathrm{d}^{2}v\wedge\mathrm{D}\hat{v}=(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}N ∫z,‖v‖=1Ω=N​∫‖v‖=1d2​v∧D​v^=(2​π​i)2​N\int_{z,\|v\|=1}\Omega=N\int_{\|v\|=1}\mathrm{d}^{2}v\wedge\mathrm{D}\hat{v}=(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}N

(3.33)


where the 3-sphere integral may be performed using standard techniques. These periods are necessarily quantized for the path integral of holomorphic Chern-Simons (2.18) to be invariant under gauge transformations with non-zero winding number. This provides us a twistorial reason for the quantization of  N NN.




We can use  \Omega_{N} ΩN\Omega_{N} to construct the backreacted spacetime geometry. As in flat space, the incidence relations (3.15) provide a diffeomorphism from the projective spinor bundle of  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 to our backreacted twistor space  Z_{N} ZNZ_{N}. Pulling back  \Omega_{N} ΩN\Omega_{N} by this diffeomorphism yields





 \Omega_{N}=\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{\sigma^{2}}\wedge\left(\mathrm{d}\sigma+\frac{N[u\,\mathrm{d}u]}{\|u\|^{4}}\right)\,. ΩN=d2​vσ2∧(d​σ+N⁡[u​d​u]‖u‖4).\Omega_{N}=\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}v}{\sigma^{2}}\wedge\left(\mathrm{d}\sigma+\frac{N[u\,\mathrm{d}u]}{\|u\|^{4}}\right)\,.

(3.34)


This is derived by expressing  \mathrm{d}w^{\dot{\alpha}} d​wα˙\mathrm{d}w^{\dot{\alpha}} in terms of  \mathrm{d}\sigma,\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}},\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{\alpha}} d​σ,d​uα˙,d​vα˙\mathrm{d}\sigma,\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}},\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{\alpha}}, so that all  \mathrm{d}v^{\dot{\alpha}} d​vα˙\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{\alpha}} dependence drops when wedging against  \mathrm{d}^{2}v d2​v\mathrm{d}^{2}v. As expected, it has a pair of second-order poles in  \sigma σ\sigma, located at  \sigma=0,\infty σ=0,∞\sigma=0,\infty in our choice of frame for the projective spinor bundle. Explicitly substituting





 \mathrm{d}^{2}v=\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{1}}\wedge\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{2}}=(\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{1}}-\sigma\,\mathrm{d}\bar{u}^{2}-\bar{u}^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma)\wedge(\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{2}}+\sigma\,\mathrm{d}\bar{u}^{1}+\bar{u}^{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma) d2​v=d​v1˙∧d​v2˙=(d​u1˙−σ​d​u¯2−u¯2​d​σ)∧(d​u2˙+σ​d​u¯1+u¯1​d​σ)\mathrm{d}^{2}v=\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{1}}\wedge\mathrm{d}v^{\dot{2}}=(\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{1}}-\sigma\,\mathrm{d}\bar{u}^{2}-\bar{u}^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma)\wedge(\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{2}}+\sigma\,\mathrm{d}\bar{u}^{1}+\bar{u}^{1}\mathrm{d}\sigma)

(3.35)


and applying the contour integral formula (2.1), we obtain the 2-form





 \omega=\frac{1}{2\pi}\oint_{\sigma=0}\Omega_{N}=\mathrm{i}\left(\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\mathrm{d}\hat{u}^{\dot{\beta}}+N\,\frac{[u\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{\|u\|^{4}}\right) ω=12​π​∮σ=0ΩN=i⁡(ϵα˙​β˙​d​uα˙∧d​u^β˙+N​[u​d​u]∧[u^​d​u^]‖u‖4)\omega=\frac{1}{2\pi}\oint_{\sigma=0}\Omega_{N}=\mathrm{i}\left(\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\mathrm{d}\hat{u}^{\dot{\beta}}+N\,\frac{[u\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{\|u\|^{4}}\right)

(3.36)


on  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0. It is hermitian and satisfies  \mathrm{d}\omega=0 d​ω=0\mathrm{d}\omega=0, so it gives the Kähler form of a hermitian metric on  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0. The Penrose transform ensures that it is a self-dual (and hence scalar-flat) metric. Introducing the holomorphic and antiholomorphic derivatives  \partial=\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}}\,\partial_{u^{\dot{\alpha}}} ∂=duα˙∂uα˙\partial=\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}}\,\partial_{u^{\dot{\alpha}}},  \bar{\partial}=\mathrm{d}\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,\partial_{\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}} ∂¯=du^α˙∂u^α˙\bar{\partial}=\mathrm{d}\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,\partial_{\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}} on  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2}, we find





 \omega=\mathrm{i}\,\partial\bar{\partial}K\,,\qquad K=\|u\|^{2}+N\log\|u\|^{2}\,. ω=i​∂∂¯​K,K=‖u‖2+N​log⁡‖u‖2.\omega=\mathrm{i}\,\partial\bar{\partial}K\,,\qquad K=\|u\|^{2}+N\log\|u\|^{2}\,.

(3.37)


In the limit  N\to 0 N→0N\to 0 of zero backreaction,  K KK reverts to the Kähler potential of the flat metric.






3.3 Burns space




Let  x^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=(u^{\dot{\alpha}},\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}) xα​α˙=(uα˙,u^α˙)x^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=(u^{\dot{\alpha}},\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}) be complex coordinates on  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 as before (see (2.6) for our conventions). The Kähler metric obtained from the Kähler form (3.36) is known as the Burns metric [31]. It is given by





 \mathrm{d}s^{2}=2\left(\|\mathrm{d}u\|^{2}+N\,\frac{|[u\,\mathrm{d}u]|^{2}}{\|u\|^{4}}\right)\,, d​s2=2​(‖d​u‖2+N​|[u​d​u]|2‖u‖4),\mathrm{d}s^{2}=2\left(\|\mathrm{d}u\|^{2}+N\,\frac{|[u\,\mathrm{d}u]|^{2}}{\|u\|^{4}}\right)\,,

(3.38)


where  |[u\,\mathrm{d}u]|^{2}=|u^{\dot{2}}\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{1}}-u^{\dot{1}}\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{2}}|^{2} |[u​d​u]|2=|u2˙​d​u1˙−u1˙​d​u2˙|2|[u\,\mathrm{d}u]|^{2}=|u^{\dot{2}}\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{1}}-u^{\dot{1}}\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{2}}|^{2} as usual. This is Riemannian for  N>0 N>0N>0.




Its isometry group is  \mathrm{U}(2) U⁡(2)\mathrm{U}(2), generated by rotations on the dotted index:





 u^{\dot{\alpha}}\mapsto L^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}u^{\dot{\beta}}\,,\qquad L\in\mathrm{U}(2)\,. uα˙↦Lα˙​uβ˙β˙,L∈U⁡(2).u^{\dot{\alpha}}\mapsto L^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}u^{\dot{\beta}}\,,\qquad L\in\mathrm{U}(2)\,.

(3.39)


This is a  \mathrm{U}(1)\times\mathrm{SU}(2) U⁡(1)×SU⁡(2)\mathrm{U}(1)\times\mathrm{SU}(2) subgroup of the  \mathrm{Spin}(4)\simeq\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{SU}(2) Spin⁡(4)≃SU⁡(2)×SU⁡(2)\mathrm{Spin}(4)\simeq\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{SU}(2) isometry group of flat space: the left-handed  \mathrm{SU}(2) SU⁡(2)\mathrm{SU}(2) is broken down to  \mathrm{U}(1) U⁡(1)\mathrm{U}(1), whereas the right-handed  \mathrm{SU}(2) SU⁡(2)\mathrm{SU}(2) remains unbroken and acts on the dotted indices in the usual way. The two  \mathrm{U}(1) U⁡(1)\mathrm{U}(1) subgroups of this isometry group generate circle actions in the  u^{\dot{1}} u1˙u^{\dot{1}} and  u^{\dot{2}} u2˙u^{\dot{2}} complex planes, each of which can be used to rewrite the Burns metric as a metric on a circle bundle over AdS3. This is reviewed later on in this section.




Following the general discussion in section 2.2 and 2.3, the type I B-model compactifies to Mabuchi gravity plus an  \mathrm{SO}(8) SO⁡(8)\mathrm{SO}(8) WZW4 model in the classical background of the Burns metric. For the purposes of holography, we would like to work in the large  N NN limit. To obtain a meaningful limit, one needs to perform the change of coordinates





 u^{\dot{\alpha}}\mapsto\sqrt{N}u^{\dot{\alpha}}\,. uα˙↦N​uα˙.u^{\dot{\alpha}}\mapsto\sqrt{N}u^{\dot{\alpha}}\,.

(3.40)


Doing this, one obtains an alternative expression for the Burns metric,





 \mathrm{d}s^{2}=2N\left(\|\mathrm{d}u\|^{2}+\frac{|[u\,\mathrm{d}u]|^{2}}{\|u\|^{4}}\right)\,. d​s2=2​N​(‖d​u‖2+|[u​d​u]|2‖u‖4).\mathrm{d}s^{2}=2N\left(\|\mathrm{d}u\|^{2}+\frac{|[u\,\mathrm{d}u]|^{2}}{\|u\|^{4}}\right)\,.

(3.41)


The corresponding Kähler potential becomes  K=N(\|u\|^{2}+\log\|u\|^{2}) K=N⁡(‖u‖2+log⁡‖u‖2)K=N(\|u\|^{2}+\log\|u\|^{2}).




In this coordinate system, the WZW4 action (including the coupling to the Kähler perturbation  \rho ρ\rho) rescales as





 S_{\text{WZW}_{4}}[\phi,\omega+\mathrm{i}\,\partial\bar{\partial}\rho]\mapsto NS_{\text{WZW}_{4}}\!\left[\phi,\omega|_{N=1}+\frac{\mathrm{i}\,\partial\bar{\partial}\rho}{N}\right] SWZW4​[ϕ,ω+i​∂∂¯​ρ]↦N​SWZW4​[ϕ,ω|N=1+i​∂∂¯​ρN]S_{\text{WZW}_{4}}[\phi,\omega+\mathrm{i}\,\partial\bar{\partial}\rho]\mapsto NS_{\text{WZW}_{4}}\!\left[\phi,\omega|_{N=1}+\frac{\mathrm{i}\,\partial\bar{\partial}\rho}{N}\right]

(3.42)


where  \omega|_{N=1} ω|N=1\omega|_{N=1} is the canonically normalized Kähler form obtained by setting  N=1 N=1N=1 in (3.36). This is the form of the action employed in our Letter [36]. The quantization of  N NN follows from the quantization of the Kähler form required by the WZW4 model. Rescaling  \phi\mapsto\phi/\sqrt{N} ϕ↦ϕ/N\phi\mapsto\phi/\sqrt{N} then turns  1/\sqrt{N} 1/N1/\sqrt{N} into the topological string coupling. The  \phi^{3} ϕ3\phi^{3} coupling becomes  1/\sqrt{N} 1/N1/\sqrt{N}, which is the open string coupling constant, while the  \rho\phi^{2} ρ​ϕ2\rho\phi^{2} coupling is  1/N 1/N1/N, the closed string coupling constant. This allows for a Green-Schwarz anomaly cancellation of the gauge and gravitational anomalies in twistor space [14, 15].




The rescaling of the Mabuchi functional can also be evaluated straightforwardly without any need to rescale  \rho ρ\rho. The Kähler form changes to  \omega\mapsto\omega|_{N=1}N ω↦ω|N=1​N\omega\mapsto\omega|_{N=1}N while the Laplacian maps to  \triangle\mapsto\triangle|_{N=1}N^{-1} △↦△|N=1​N−1\triangle\mapsto\triangle|_{N=1}N^{-1} (see (7.2)), so the kinetic term in (2.49) immediately becomes independent of  N NN. The  \rho^{3} ρ3\rho^{3} coupling is seen to be  1/N 1/N1/N as expected.




Ideally, this is the form of the action one should use to take the large  N NN limit, but in what follows we will continue to work with the original metric (3.38). This will prove useful since most of our calculations will turn out to be expressible as formal power series in  N NN. This does not cause any trouble with taking a large  N NN limit because to work in the large  N NN limit simply means working at tree level in the bulk in most scenarios of interest.




For now, let us continue to explore the geometry associated to (3.38). The square root of the determinant of the Burns metric is





 \sqrt{|g|}=1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\,. |g|=1+N‖u‖2.\sqrt{|g|}=1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\,.

(3.43)


Using this, we can compute its Ricci form





 \begin{split}P&=-\mathrm{i}\,\partial\bar{\partial}\log\sqrt{|g|}\\
&=\frac{\mathrm{i}N}{\|u\|^{2}(N+\|u\|^{2})}\left(\frac{[u\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{\|u\|^{2}}+\frac{[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[u\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{N+\|u\|^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split} P=−i∂∂¯log|g|=i​N‖u‖2​(N+‖u‖2)​([u​d​u]∧[u^​d​u^]‖u‖2+[u^​d​u]∧[u​d​u^]N+‖u‖2).\begin{split}P&=-\mathrm{i}\,\partial\bar{\partial}\log\sqrt{|g|}\\
&=\frac{\mathrm{i}N}{\|u\|^{2}(N+\|u\|^{2})}\left(\frac{[u\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{\|u\|^{2}}+\frac{[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[u\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{N+\|u\|^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(3.44)


This is non-vanishing, so the metric is not Ricci-flat as expected. On the other hand, noticing that the Kähler form (3.36) can be reexpressed as





 \omega=\mathrm{i}\left(1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{[u\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{\|u\|^{2}}-\frac{[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[u\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{N+\|u\|^{2}}\right)\,, ω=i⁡(1+N‖u‖2)​([u​d​u]∧[u^​d​u^]‖u‖2−[u^​d​u]∧[u​d​u^]N+‖u‖2),\omega=\mathrm{i}\left(1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{[u\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{\|u\|^{2}}-\frac{[\hat{u}\,\mathrm{d}u]\wedge[u\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{N+\|u\|^{2}}\right)\,,

(3.45)


it becomes immediate that the Ricci form satisfies  \omega\wedge P=0 ω∧P=0\omega\wedge P=0. This confirms that the Burns metric is scalar-flat, i.e., has Ricci scalar  R=0 R=0R=0. Importantly, this tells us that the Burns metric has cosmological constant  \Lambda=0 Λ=0\Lambda=0,1313
              13
              
              
              
            As opposed to the more general possibility of SD metrics of constant scalar curvature  R=24\Lambda\neq 0 R=24​Λ≠0R=24\Lambda\neq 0. distinguishing it from the asymptotically AdS geometries obtained from backreaction in past holographic setups.




The singularity at the origin  u^{\dot{\alpha}}=0 uα˙=0u^{\dot{\alpha}}=0 is not a genuine curvature singularity, as is easily corroborated by computing the squared Ricci tensor or the Kretschmann scalar,





 R^{\mu\nu}R_{\mu\nu}=\frac{4N^{2}}{(N+\|u\|^{2})^{4}}\,,\qquad R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=\frac{32N^{2}}{(N+\|u\|^{2})^{4}}\,, Rμ​ν​Rμ​ν=4​N2(N+‖u‖2)4,Rμ​ν​ρ​σ​Rμ​ν​ρ​σ=32​N2(N+‖u‖2)4,R^{\mu\nu}R_{\mu\nu}=\frac{4N^{2}}{(N+\|u\|^{2})^{4}}\,,\qquad R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=\frac{32N^{2}}{(N+\|u\|^{2})^{4}}\,,

(3.46)


which are both finite at the origin. It simply signals that the spacetime is incomplete.




In fact, we can easily extend (3.38) from a metric on  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 to a metric on  \widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} ℂ~2\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2}, the blow-up of  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2} at the origin, by identifying  \widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} ℂ~2\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} with the total space of the tautological line bundle  \mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathbb{CP}^{1} 𝒪⁡(−1)→ℂ​ℙ1\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathbb{CP}^{1}. To see this, let  \zeta^{\dot{\alpha}} ζα˙\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}} denote homogeneous coordinates on the base  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1}. And let  t\in\mathbb{C} t∈ℂt\in\mathbb{C} be a holomorphic coordinate along the fibers of  \mathscr{O}(-1) 𝒪⁡(−1)\mathscr{O}(-1). We can perform the coordinate transformation





 u^{\dot{\alpha}}=t\,\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}} uα˙=t​ζα˙u^{\dot{\alpha}}=t\,\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}

(3.47)


which is bijective for  u^{\dot{\alpha}}\neq 0 uα˙≠0u^{\dot{\alpha}}\neq 0 and maps to the exceptional divisor  \{t=0 {t=0\{t=0,  [\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{1}\} [ζα˙]∈ℂℙ1}[\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{1}\} at the origin  u^{\dot{\alpha}}=0 uα˙=0u^{\dot{\alpha}}=0. Under non-zero rescalings  \zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}\mapsto s\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}} ζα˙↦s​ζα˙\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}\mapsto s\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}}, the fiber coordinate transforms as  t\mapsto s^{-1}t t↦s−1​tt\mapsto s^{-1}t and  u^{\dot{\alpha}} uα˙u^{\dot{\alpha}} remains invariant. Note that since  t=0 t=0t=0 is the origin of  \mathbb{C}^{2} ℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2}, the metric (3.38) is naively ill-defined there. But since  [\zeta\,\zeta]=0 [ζ​ζ]=0[\zeta\,\zeta]=0, one finds that  [u\,\mathrm{d}u]=t\,[\zeta\,\mathrm{d}\zeta] [u​d​u]=t⁡[ζ​d​ζ][u\,\mathrm{d}u]=t\,[\zeta\,\mathrm{d}\zeta], so the substitution (3.47) converts (3.38) into





 \mathrm{d}s^{2}=2\left(\|\zeta\,\mathrm{d}t+t\,\mathrm{d}\zeta\|^{2}+N\,\frac{|[\zeta\,\mathrm{d}\zeta]|^{2}}{\|\zeta\|^{4}}\right)\,. d​s2=2​(‖ζ​d​t+t​d​ζ‖2+N​|[ζ​d​ζ]|2‖ζ‖4).\mathrm{d}s^{2}=2\left(\|\zeta\,\mathrm{d}t+t\,\mathrm{d}\zeta\|^{2}+N\,\frac{|[\zeta\,\mathrm{d}\zeta]|^{2}}{\|\zeta\|^{4}}\right)\,.

(3.48)


Since  t tt drops out of the second term, this resulting metric is non-singular at the locus  t=0 t=0t=0. Hence, we can extend the metric freely to  t=0 t=0t=0 and thus to the total space of  \mathscr{O}(-1) 𝒪⁡(−1)\mathscr{O}(-1).




Let  \mathrm{D}\zeta\vcentcolon=[\zeta\,\mathrm{d}\zeta] D​ζ:=[ζ​d​ζ]\mathrm{D}\zeta\vcentcolon=[\zeta\,\mathrm{d}\zeta] denote the standard weight  2 22 frame for the holomorphic cotangent bundle of  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1}. We can complete this into a frame  \mathrm{D}\zeta,\mathrm{D}t D​ζ,D​t\mathrm{D}\zeta,\mathrm{D}t for the holomorphic cotangent bundle of  \mathscr{O}(-1) 𝒪⁡(−1)\mathscr{O}(-1) by using the standard Chern connection 1-form on  \mathscr{O}(-1) 𝒪⁡(−1)\mathscr{O}(-1),





 \mathrm{D}t\vcentcolon=\mathrm{d}t+\frac{[\hat{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta]}{\|\zeta\|^{2}}\,t\,. D​t:=d​t+[ζ^​d​ζ]‖ζ‖2​t.\mathrm{D}t\vcentcolon=\mathrm{d}t+\frac{[\hat{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta]}{\|\zeta\|^{2}}\,t\,.

(3.49)


This frame is designed to be conformally covariant under local non-zero rescalings:





 (\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}},t)\mapsto\left(s(\zeta,t)\,\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}},\frac{t}{s(\zeta,t)}\right)\implies(\mathrm{D}\zeta,\mathrm{D}t)\mapsto\left(s(\zeta,t)^{2}\,\mathrm{D}\zeta,\frac{\mathrm{D}t}{s(\zeta,t)}\right)\,. (ζα˙,t)↦(s⁡(ζ,t)​ζα˙,ts⁡(ζ,t))⟹(D​ζ,D​t)↦(s​(ζ,t)2​D​ζ,D​ts⁡(ζ,t)).(\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}},t)\mapsto\left(s(\zeta,t)\,\zeta^{\dot{\alpha}},\frac{t}{s(\zeta,t)}\right)\implies(\mathrm{D}\zeta,\mathrm{D}t)\mapsto\left(s(\zeta,t)^{2}\,\mathrm{D}\zeta,\frac{\mathrm{D}t}{s(\zeta,t)}\right)\,.

(3.50)


The metric (3.48) can then be compactly expressed as





 \mathrm{d}s^{2}=2\left\{\|\zeta\|^{2}|\mathrm{D}t|^{2}+\left(N+|t|^{2}\|\zeta\|^{2}\right)\frac{|\mathrm{D}\zeta|^{2}}{\|\zeta\|^{4}}\right\}\,. d​s2=2​{‖ζ‖2​|D​t|2+(N+|t|2​‖ζ‖2)​|D​ζ|2‖ζ‖4}.\mathrm{d}s^{2}=2\left\{\|\zeta\|^{2}|\mathrm{D}t|^{2}+\left(N+|t|^{2}\|\zeta\|^{2}\right)\frac{|\mathrm{D}\zeta|^{2}}{\|\zeta\|^{4}}\right\}\,.

(3.51)


It is seen to be invariant under the local rescalings (3.50), so it is well-defined everywhere on  \mathscr{O}(-1) 𝒪⁡(−1)\mathscr{O}(-1).




In our analysis, we have been slightly cavalier about length dimensions by viewing both  u^{\dot{\alpha}} uα˙u^{\dot{\alpha}} and  N NN as dimensionless. We can restore standard length dimensions for the coordinates  u^{\dot{\alpha}} uα˙u^{\dot{\alpha}} by replacing  N NN by a length scale  N\ell^{2} N​ℓ2N\ell^{2}. Here,  \ell\sim\sqrt{\alpha^{\prime}} ℓ∼α′\ell\sim\sqrt{\alpha^{\prime}} is essentially the string length and is physically interpreted as setting the size of the blow-up at the origin. As  t\to 0 t→0t\to 0 with  N>0 N>0N>0, (3.51) reduces to  N/2 N/2N/2 times the usual stereographic metric  4|\mathrm{D}\zeta|^{2}/\|\zeta\|^{4} 4​|D​ζ|2/‖ζ‖44|\mathrm{D}\zeta|^{2}/\|\zeta\|^{4} on the exceptional divisor  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1}. Upon changing  N\mapsto N\ell^{2} N↦N​ℓ2N\mapsto N\ell^{2}, this shows that the radius of the exceptional divisor is precisely  \sqrt{\frac{N}{2}}\,\ell N2​ℓ\sqrt{\frac{N}{2}}\,\ell. The limit  N\to 0 N→0N\to 0 of zero backreaction is equivalent to turning off the blow-up. Since the topological string is classically exact in  \alpha^{\prime} α′\alpha^{\prime}, we will suppress  \ell ℓ\ell consistently throughout this work by setting it to 1.




The Burns metric is a complete, asymptotically flat, zero scalar curvature metric on  \widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} ℂ~2\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} [31, 72, 73], and our discussion of its twistor space has been adapted from the more extensive discussion presented in [30]. We conclude that the completed spacetime geometry obtained from D1 brane backreaction in the twistorial B-model is that of  \widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} ℂ~2\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2} equipped with this metric. We refer to it variously as Burns space or Burns geometry.



Asymptotic flatness.




In Euclidean signature, a metric on a connected, non-compact 4-manifold  M MM is called asymptotically flat (more appropriately asymptotically Euclidean) if it asymptotes to the Euclidean metric with a falloff of at least  \mathrm{O}(r^{-2}) O⁡(r−2)\mathrm{O}(r^{-2}) with respect to some 4-radius  r=\sqrt{x^{\mu}x_{\mu}} r=xμ​xμr=\sqrt{x^{\mu}x_{\mu}}. More precisely, suppose there exists a compact set  K\subset M K⊂MK\subset M such that  M-K M−KM-K is diffeomorphic to the complement of a closed ball in  \mathbb{R}^{4} ℝ4\mathbb{R}^{4}. Let  x^{\mu} xμx^{\mu} denote coordinates on this  \mathbb{R}^{4} ℝ4\mathbb{R}^{4}. If we can find a large enough  K KK for which the metric components in these coordinates have the falloff





 g_{\mu\nu}=\delta_{\mu\nu}+\mathrm{O}(r^{-2})\,, gμ​ν=δμ​ν+O⁡(r−2),g_{\mu\nu}=\delta_{\mu\nu}+\mathrm{O}(r^{-2})\,,

(3.52)


then the metric  g gg is said to be asymptotically flat [30, 56]. This property is manifest for the Burns metric in the complex coordinates  u^{\dot{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 uα˙∈ℂ2−0u^{\dot{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 employed in this section, as its difference  2Nu_{\dot{\alpha}}\hat{u}_{\dot{\beta}}\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}\hat{u}^{\dot{\beta}}/\|u\|^{4} 2​N​uα˙​u^β˙​d​uα˙​d​u^β˙/‖u‖42Nu_{\dot{\alpha}}\hat{u}_{\dot{\beta}}\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}\hat{u}^{\dot{\beta}}/\|u\|^{4} from the flat metric has the componentwise falloff  \mathrm{O}(\|u\|^{2}/\|u\|^{4})=\mathrm{O}(\|u\|^{-2}) O⁡(‖u‖2/‖u‖4)=O⁡(‖u‖−2)\mathrm{O}(\|u\|^{2}/\|u\|^{4})=\mathrm{O}(\|u\|^{-2}).




Relation to  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} and spacetime foam.




At the level of the topology of spacetime, the backreaction has precipitated a geometric transition from flat space  \mathbb{C}^{2}\simeq\mathbb{R}^{4} ℂ2≃ℝ4\mathbb{C}^{2}\simeq\mathbb{R}^{4} to its blow-up at the origin  \widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\simeq\mathscr{O}(-1) ℂ~2≃𝒪⁡(−1)\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\simeq\mathscr{O}(-1)! In fact, it was already conjectured in the early work on twistor strings [41] that wrapping D1 branes on twistor lines corresponds to blowing up the corresponding points in spacetime; our results give a concrete realization of the simplest case of this proposal. The work of [41] was originally motivated from the subject of spacetime foam, where  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} plays a key role.




The Burns metric is seen to be conformal to the Fubini-Study metric on  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} by performing the inversion  u^{\dot{\alpha}}\mapsto\sqrt{N}u^{\dot{\alpha}}/\|u\|^{2} uα˙↦N​uα˙/‖u‖2u^{\dot{\alpha}}\mapsto\sqrt{N}u^{\dot{\alpha}}/\|u\|^{2} [73],





 \begin{split}\mathrm{d}s^{2}_{\text{Burns}}&\mapsto 2N\left(1+\frac{1}{\|u\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{\|\mathrm{d}u\|^{2}+|[u\,\mathrm{d}u]|^{2}}{(1+\|u\|^{2})^{2}}\\
&=2N\left(1+\frac{1}{\|u\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s^{2}_{\text{Fubini-Study}}\,.\end{split} d​sBurns2↦2​N​(1+1‖u‖2)2​‖d​u‖2+|[u​d​u]|2(1+‖u‖2)2=2​N​(1+1‖u‖2)2​d​sFubini-Study2.\begin{split}\mathrm{d}s^{2}_{\text{Burns}}&\mapsto 2N\left(1+\frac{1}{\|u\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{\|\mathrm{d}u\|^{2}+|[u\,\mathrm{d}u]|^{2}}{(1+\|u\|^{2})^{2}}\\
&=2N\left(1+\frac{1}{\|u\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s^{2}_{\text{Fubini-Study}}\,.\end{split}

(3.53)


The conformal factor is clearly  \sqrt{2N}\,(1+\|u\|^{-2}) 2​N​(1+‖u‖−2)\sqrt{2N}\,(1+\|u\|^{-2}). Though we are working in an affine patch, one can check that this conformal transformation extends everywhere on  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} except for its singularity  u^{\dot{\alpha}}=0 uα˙=0u^{\dot{\alpha}}=0. Introducing homogeneous coordinates  [U^{k}]=[u^{\dot{\alpha}},1] [Uk]=[uα˙,1][U^{k}]=[u^{\dot{\alpha}},1] on  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2}, this is seen to be the point  [0,0,1] [0,0,1][0,0,1], which one may interpret as the “point at infinity”. We conclude that Burns space is conformally diffeomorphic to  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} minus a point equipped with its Fubini-Study metric.1414
                14
                
                
                
              It is easily checked that  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} minus a point is biholomorphic to  \mathscr{O}(1) 𝒪⁡(1)\mathscr{O}(1) in an orientation-preserving manner. The diffeomorphism to  \mathscr{O}(-1) 𝒪⁡(−1)\mathscr{O}(-1) – the dual to  \mathscr{O}(1) 𝒪⁡(1)\mathscr{O}(1) – is instead orientation-reversing and not holomorphic.




Historically, because of this fact, Burns space made an appearance in models of spacetime foam [32, 74]. These works studied Euclidean analogues of scattering amplitudes on gravitational instantons like  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2}. In particular, they studied conformally coupled scalars on  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2}. Due to the conformal equivalence between  \mathbb{CP}^{2} ℂ​ℙ2\mathbb{CP}^{2} and Burns space, some of the results of their perturbative analysis will beautifully carry over to our context modulo appropriate conformal rescalings.




In the rest of this section, we review some further interesting properties of Burns space which are potentially relevant to celestial holography but can be safely skipped on first reading.




Realization as an Einstein-Maxwell instanton.




We have taken the viewpoint that the Burns metric is a classical background of Mabuchi gravity. It has a non-zero Ricci form (3.44) so is clearly not Ricci-flat, therefore it does not represent a purely gravitational instanton in the usual sense. Since Burns space is asymptotically flat, this is consistent with the observation of [75] that any finite action, SD Ricci-flat spacetime that is asymptotically Euclidean must be  \mathbb{R}^{4} ℝ4\mathbb{R}^{4}. Nonetheless, the Burns metric (and more generally any scalar-flat Kähler metric) does act as an Einstein-Maxwell instanton [76, 77], supported by the non-self-dual Maxwell flux





 F=\omega+P F=ω+PF=\omega+P

(3.54)


where  \omega ω\omega and  P PP are its Kähler and Ricci forms given in (3.36) and (3.44) respectively.




To see this, let us compute the Riemann curvature of Burns space. Introduce a complex orthonormal coframe  \theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} θα​α˙\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} for the Burns metric,1515
                15
                
                
                
              Such complexified coframes are convenient to work with but do not obey the reality conditions (A.3) assumed in appendix A. If needed, they can always be rotated to ones that do by complexified local Lorentz transformations on the spinor indices.





 \theta^{1\dot{\alpha}}=\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}}-\frac{N\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|u\|^{4}}\,[u\,\mathrm{d}u]\,,\qquad\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}}=\mathrm{d}\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,. θ1​α˙=d​uα˙−N​u^α˙‖u‖4​[u​d​u],θ2​α˙=d​u^α˙.\theta^{1\dot{\alpha}}=\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{\alpha}}-\frac{N\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}}{\|u\|^{4}}\,[u\,\mathrm{d}u]\,,\qquad\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}}=\mathrm{d}\hat{u}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,.

(3.55)


It satisfies  \mathrm{d}s^{2}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\,\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\,\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}} d​s2=ϵα​β​ϵα˙​β˙​θα​α˙​θβ​β˙\mathrm{d}s^{2}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\,\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\,\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}. It is standard to use  \Sigma^{\alpha\beta}=\theta^{\alpha}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\theta^{\beta\dot{\alpha}} Σα​β=θαα˙∧θβ​α˙\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}=\theta^{\alpha}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\theta^{\beta\dot{\alpha}} and  \tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=\theta_{\alpha}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\theta^{\alpha\dot{\beta}} Σ~α˙​β˙=θαα˙∧θα​β˙\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=\theta_{\alpha}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\theta^{\alpha\dot{\beta}} as the bases of ASD and SD 2-forms on spacetime [78], see appendix A for more details. The ASD spin connection is determined from  \mathrm{d}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}=2\,\Gamma^{(\alpha}{}_{\gamma}\wedge\Sigma^{\beta)\gamma} dΣα​β=2Γ(αCLOSEγ∧ΣOPENβ)​γ\mathrm{d}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}=2\,\Gamma^{(\alpha}{}_{\gamma}\wedge\Sigma^{\beta)\gamma},





 \Gamma_{12}=\Gamma_{21}=\frac{N[u\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{2\,\|u\|^{2}(N+\|u\|^{2})}\,,\qquad\Gamma_{11}=\Gamma_{22}=0\,. Γ12=Γ21=N⁡[u​d​u^]2​‖u‖2​(N+‖u‖2),Γ11=Γ22=0.\Gamma_{12}=\Gamma_{21}=\frac{N[u\,\mathrm{d}\hat{u}]}{2\,\|u\|^{2}(N+\|u\|^{2})}\,,\qquad\Gamma_{11}=\Gamma_{22}=0\,.

(3.56)


The associated ASD Riemann curvature 2-form is easily confirmed to be pure Ricci and self-dual





 \begin{split}&R_{\alpha\beta}=\mathrm{d}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}+\Gamma_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}\wedge\Gamma_{\gamma\beta}=\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,,\\
&\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=\frac{No_{(\alpha}\iota_{\beta)}u_{(\dot{\alpha}}\hat{u}_{\dot{\beta})}}{\|u\|^{2}(N+\|u\|^{2})^{2}}\,.\end{split} Rα​β=dΓα​β+Γαγ∧Γγ​β=Φα​β​α˙​β˙Σ~α˙​β˙,Φα​β​α˙​β˙=N​o(αCLOSE​ιOPENβ)​u(α˙CLOSE​u^OPENβ˙)‖u‖2​(N+‖u‖2)2.\begin{split}&R_{\alpha\beta}=\mathrm{d}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}+\Gamma_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}\wedge\Gamma_{\gamma\beta}=\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,,\\
&\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=\frac{No_{(\alpha}\iota_{\beta)}u_{(\dot{\alpha}}\hat{u}_{\dot{\beta})}}{\|u\|^{2}(N+\|u\|^{2})^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(3.57)


Here,  o_{\alpha}=(1,0) oα=(1,0)o_{\alpha}=(1,0) and  \iota_{\alpha}=(0,1) ια=(0,1)\iota_{\alpha}=(0,1) are a constant spinor basis. This gives another proof of the fact that the metric is self-dual and scalar-flat.




The Einstein-Maxwell equations read






 \displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R=T_{\mu\nu}=F_{\mu\rho}F_{\nu}{}^{\rho}-\frac{1}{4}g_{\mu\nu}F^{2}\,, Rμ​ν−12gμ​νR=Tμ​ν=Fμ​ρFνρ−14gμ​νF2,\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R=T_{\mu\nu}=F_{\mu\rho}F_{\nu}{}^{\rho}-\frac{1}{4}g_{\mu\nu}F^{2}\,,

(3.58)




 \displaystyle\mathrm{d}F=\mathrm{d}\star F=0\,, d​F=d⋆F=0,\displaystyle\mathrm{d}F=\mathrm{d}\star F=0\,,

(3.59)


where  R_{\mu\nu} Rμ​νR_{\mu\nu} is the Ricci tensor,  F FF is the Maxwell field strength, and  \star ⋆\star is the Hodge dual. Decompose  F FF into ASD and SD parts,  F=F_{\alpha\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}+\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} F=Fα​β​Σα​β+F~α˙​β˙​Σ~α˙​β˙F=F_{\alpha\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}+\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}. In an orthonormal frame like ours, the spinor equivalent of the Ricci tensor decomposes as  R_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta\dot{\beta}}=2\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}R Rα​α˙​β​β˙=2​Φα​β​α˙​β˙−14​ϵα​β​ϵα˙​β˙​RR_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta\dot{\beta}}=2\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}R [79], whereas that of the Maxwell stress tensor reduces to  T_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta\dot{\beta}}=-2F_{\alpha\beta}\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} Tα​α˙​β​β˙=−2​Fα​β​F~α˙​β˙T_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta\dot{\beta}}=-2F_{\alpha\beta}\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}. The Einstein equation (3.58) then decomposes into two irreducible equations





 \Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}+F_{\alpha\beta}\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=0\,,\qquad R=0\,. Φα​β​α˙​β˙+Fα​β​F~α˙​β˙=0,R=0.\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}+F_{\alpha\beta}\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=0\,,\qquad R=0\,.

(3.60)


Now, in our orientation with volume form  \sqrt{|g|}\,\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{1}}\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{u}^{1}\wedge\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{2}}\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{u}^{2} |g|​d​u1˙∧d​u¯1∧d​u2˙∧d​u¯2\sqrt{|g|}\,\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{1}}\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{u}^{1}\wedge\mathrm{d}u^{\dot{2}}\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{u}^{2}, the Kähler form  \omega ω\omega is ASD, a standard fact in Kähler geometry. On the other hand, the Ricci form  P PP is SD, which can be checked directly but is mainly a consequence of scalar-flatness. Setting  F=\omega+P F=ω+PF=\omega+P, we find





 \begin{split}F_{\alpha\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}&=\omega\implies F_{\alpha\beta}=\mathrm{i}\,o_{(\alpha}\iota_{\beta)}\,,\\
\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}&=P\implies\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}Nu_{(\dot{\alpha}}\hat{u}_{\dot{\beta})}}{\|u\|^{2}(N+\|u\|^{2})^{2}}\,.\end{split} Fα​β​Σα​β=ω⟹Fα​β=i​o(αCLOSE​ιOPENβ),F~α˙​β˙​Σ~α˙​β˙=P⟹F~α˙​β˙=i​N​u(α˙CLOSE​u^OPENβ˙)‖u‖2​(N+‖u‖2)2.\begin{split}F_{\alpha\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}&=\omega\implies F_{\alpha\beta}=\mathrm{i}\,o_{(\alpha}\iota_{\beta)}\,,\\
\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}&=P\implies\tilde{F}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}Nu_{(\dot{\alpha}}\hat{u}_{\dot{\beta})}}{\|u\|^{2}(N+\|u\|^{2})^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(3.61)


The equations (3.60) are then immediately satisfied by (3.57) and (3.61) by construction. At the same time,  F=\omega+P F=ω+PF=\omega+P solves the Maxwell equations (3.59) because  \omega ω\omega and  P PP are co-closed by virtue of being closed and either ASD or SD.




This shows that Burns space can be supported within Einstein gravity via a Maxwell flux. The Maxwell background (3.61) is not self-dual, as  F_{\alpha\beta}\neq 0 Fα​β≠0F_{\alpha\beta}\neq 0. So this is necessarily a solution of the non-self-dual theory. Nonetheless, this fact has been used for generating new solutions of supergravity in the works [80, 81], which may be of interest for building more general setups for flat holography.




Hyperbolic foliation.




The Burns metric also admits other geometrical incarnations. One that may be of potential interest for holography is as a metric on the total space of a circle bundle over an open subset of Euclidean AdS3 [30].




This can be understood by a judicious change of coordinates. For simplicity, let us revert to working on  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0. The Burns metric has a pair of circle isometries: rotations in the  u^{\dot{1}} u1˙u^{\dot{1}} complex plane or the  u^{\dot{2}} u2˙u^{\dot{2}} complex plane. In the symplectic structure associated to the Kähler form  \omega ω\omega, both of these are generated by hamiltonian vector fields. Introduce new coordinates  t\in(0,2\pi) t∈(0,2​π)t\in(0,2\pi),  q\in(0,\infty) q∈(0,∞)q\in(0,\infty),  (x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2} (x,y)∈ℝ2(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2} defined by the relations





 t=\arg(u^{\dot{1}})\,,\qquad q^{2}=2\,|u^{\dot{1}}|^{2}\left(1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\right),\qquad u^{\dot{2}}=\frac{x+\mathrm{i}y}{\sqrt{2}}\,. t=arg⁡(u1˙),q2=2​|u1˙|2​(1+N‖u‖2),u2˙=x+i​y2.t=\arg(u^{\dot{1}})\,,\qquad q^{2}=2\,|u^{\dot{1}}|^{2}\left(1+\frac{N}{\|u\|^{2}}\right),\qquad u^{\dot{2}}=\frac{x+\mathrm{i}y}{\sqrt{2}}\,.

(3.62)


 t tt provides a coordinate along the orbits of the circle action rotating the  u^{\dot{1}} u1˙u^{\dot{1}} complex plane, and  q^{2} q2q^{2} is precisely the Hamiltonian generating this circle action. These coordinates are well-defined away from the fixed point set of this circle action. The latter is a copy of  \mathbb{R}^{2} ℝ2\mathbb{R}^{2} given by  \{u^{\dot{1}}=0\} {u1˙=0}\{u^{\dot{1}}=0\}. The coordinate patch on  \mathbb{C}^{2}-0 ℂ2−0\mathbb{C}^{2}-0 obtained from removing this  \mathbb{R}^{2} ℝ2\mathbb{R}^{2} (which in particular includes the origin) is  \mathbb{R}^{4}-\mathbb{R}^{2}\simeq\text{AdS}_{3}\times S^{1} ℝ4−ℝ2≃AdS3×S1\mathbb{R}^{4}-\mathbb{R}^{2}\simeq\text{AdS}_{3}\times S^{1}. The phase  t tt acts as a coordinate along  S^{1} S1S^{1}, while  (x,y,q) (x,y,q)(x,y,q) provide Poincaré coordinates on AdS3. Actually,  q qq only covers the full range  (0,\infty) (0,∞)(0,\infty) when  (x,y)\neq 0 (x,y)≠0(x,y)\neq 0. When  (x,y)=0 (x,y)=0(x,y)=0, one sees from its definition that  q^{2}=2(|u^{\dot{1}}|^{2}+N)>2N q2=2​(|u1˙|2+N)>2​Nq^{2}=2(|u^{\dot{1}}|^{2}+N)>2N. So this coordinate patch covers  S^{1} S1S^{1} times the open subset  \text{AdS}_{3}-\{x=y=0,q\leq N\} AdS3−{x=y=0,q≤N}\text{AdS}_{3}-\{x=y=0,q\leq N\}.




In these coordinates, the Burns metric (3.38) transforms into





 \mathrm{d}s^{2}=q^{2}\left(\frac{1}{V}\left(\mathrm{d}t+A\right)^{2}+V\,\frac{\mathrm{d}x^{2}+\mathrm{d}y^{2}+\mathrm{d}q^{2}}{q^{2}}\right)\,. d​s2=q2​(1V​(d​t+A)2+V​d​x2+d​y2+d​q2q2).\mathrm{d}s^{2}=q^{2}\left(\frac{1}{V}\left(\mathrm{d}t+A\right)^{2}+V\,\frac{\mathrm{d}x^{2}+\mathrm{d}y^{2}+\mathrm{d}q^{2}}{q^{2}}\right)\,.

(3.63)


The overall conformal factor of  q^{2} q2q^{2} ensures asymptotic flatness. The data  (V,A) (V,A)(V,A) is independent of  t tt and describes a magnetic monopole on AdS3 centered at  (x,y,q)=(0,0,\sqrt{2N}) (x,y,q)=(0,0,2​N)(x,y,q)=(0,0,\sqrt{2N}). The scalar potential  V VV of this monopole is found to be





 V=1+\frac{1}{2}\,\bigg(\frac{q^{2}+x^{2}+y^{2}+2N}{\sqrt{(q^{2}+x^{2}+y^{2}+2N)^{2}-8Nq^{2}}}-1\bigg)\,. V=1+12​(q2+x2+y2+2​N(q2+x2+y2+2​N)2−8​N​q2−1).V=1+\frac{1}{2}\,\bigg(\frac{q^{2}+x^{2}+y^{2}+2N}{\sqrt{(q^{2}+x^{2}+y^{2}+2N)^{2}-8Nq^{2}}}-1\bigg)\,.

(3.64)


It solves the AdS3 Laplace equation and approaches  V\to 1 V→1V\to 1 in the flat limit  N\to 0 N→0N\to 0. Recalling the expression for the hyperbolic distance between the points  (x,y,q) (x,y,q)(x,y,q) and  (0,0,\sqrt{2N}) (0,0,2​N)(0,0,\sqrt{2N}),





 \beta=\cosh^{-1}\!\left(\frac{x^{2}+y^{2}+q^{2}+2N}{2q\sqrt{2N}}\right)\,, β=cosh−1⁡(x2+y2+q2+2​N2​q​2​N),\beta=\cosh^{-1}\!\left(\frac{x^{2}+y^{2}+q^{2}+2N}{2q\sqrt{2N}}\right)\,,

(3.65)


we can express  V VV in terms of the fundamental solution  1/(\mathrm{e}^{2\beta}-1) 1/(e2​β−1)1/(\mathrm{e}^{2\beta}-1) of the AdS3 Laplace equation,





 V=1+\frac{1}{\mathrm{e}^{2\beta}-1}\,. V=1+1e2​β−1.V=1+\frac{1}{\mathrm{e}^{2\beta}-1}\,.

(3.66)


The associated 3-dimensional vector potential reads





 A=\frac{(x\,\mathrm{d}y-y\,\mathrm{d}x)}{2(x^{2}+y^{2})}\,\bigg(\frac{q^{2}+x^{2}+y^{2}-2N}{\sqrt{(q^{2}+x^{2}+y^{2}+2N)^{2}-8Nq^{2}}}-1\bigg)\,. A=(x​d​y−y​d​x)2​(x2+y2)​(q2+x2+y2−2​N(q2+x2+y2+2​N)2−8​N​q2−1).A=\frac{(x\,\mathrm{d}y-y\,\mathrm{d}x)}{2(x^{2}+y^{2})}\,\bigg(\frac{q^{2}+x^{2}+y^{2}-2N}{\sqrt{(q^{2}+x^{2}+y^{2}+2N)^{2}-8Nq^{2}}}-1\bigg)\,.

(3.67)


This satisfies  \mathrm{d}A=\star_{\text{AdS}_{3}}\mathrm{d}V dA=⋆AdS3dV\mathrm{d}A=\star_{\text{AdS}_{3}}\mathrm{d}V and vanishes as  N\to 0 N→0N\to 0.




In this sense, the Burns metric and its multicentered cousins found by LeBrun in [30] act as hyperbolic analogues of the Gibbons-Hawking metrics [82, 83]. This form of the metric may be helpful in relating holography on Burns space to more standard AdS3/CFT2 dualities, potentially realizing the hopes of [84]. It could also help in finding multicentered generalizations of asymptotically flat holography, perhaps in analogy with recent generalizations of twisted holography that include multiple stacks of D1 branes [85].







4 The holographic dual chiral algebra




In this section, we describe the celestial holographic dual to the B-model on the twistor space of Burns space. By compactifying the latter along twistor lines, one obtains a duality relating WZW4 + Mabuchi gravity on Burns space to this celestial CFT.




The dual CFT is a purely chiral system, living on  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} with two defects placed at  z=0 z=0z=0 and  z=\infty z=∞z=\infty. We will first describe the system without the defects.




The chiral algebra is the algebra living on  N NN D1 branes in the type I topological B-string. This algebra is an orientifold of the chiral algebra living on D1 branes in the type II topological B-string, which is the algebra studied in [16]. The orientifold procedure was computed in [14].




An alternative description of the chiral algebra is that it is the algebra associated by the superconformal localization procedure of [43, 86] to the family of 4d  \mathcal{N}=2 𝒩=2\mathcal{N}=2 supersymmetric gauge theories with  \mathrm{Sp}(N) Sp⁡(N)\mathrm{Sp}(N) gauge symmetry,  \mathrm{SO}(8) SO⁡(8)\mathrm{SO}(8) flavour symmetry and matter as described below. These are non-unitary chiral algebras with negative Kac-Moody levels and therefore, by the usual Sugawara relation, negative central charges.




The algebra is built by performing BRST reduction on a collection of free symplectic bosons. The gauge group for the BRST reduction is  \mathrm{Sp}(N) Sp⁡(N)\mathrm{Sp}(N).




Let  V VV denote the fundamental representation of dimension  2N 2​N2N, and let  i ii denote an index for a basis of  V VV. The symplectic pairing is  \omega_{ij} ωi​j\omega_{ij}.1616
            16
            
            
            
          This is not to be confused with the Kähler potential. The fundamental fields of the dual are symplectic bosons carrying conformal weights  (\frac{1}{2},0) (12,0)(\frac{1}{2},0) each:





 \begin{split}I_{ri}&\in\Omega^{\frac{1}{2},0}(\mathbb{CP}^{1},\mathbb{C}^{8}\otimes V)\\

X^{\dot{\alpha}}_{ij}&\in\Omega^{\frac{1}{2},0}(\mathbb{CP}^{1},\wedge^{2}_{0}V\otimes\mathbb{C}^{2}).\end{split}

(4.1)


The fields  X^{\dot{\alpha}} Xα˙X^{\dot{\alpha}} transform under the 4d Lorentz group as right-handed spinors. They describe D1-D1 strings. The notation  \wedge^{2}_{0}V ∧20V\wedge^{2}_{0}V means we are using the trace-free exterior square, so that





 \omega^{ij}X^{\dot{\alpha}}_{ij}=0\,. ωi​j​Xi​jα˙=0.\omega^{ij}X^{\dot{\alpha}}_{ij}=0\,.

(4.2)


 \omega^{ij} ωi​j\omega^{ij} here is the inverse of  \omega_{ij} ωi​j\omega_{ij}, with the convention  \omega^{ij}\omega_{jk}=\delta^{i}_{k} ωi​j​ωj​k=δki\omega^{ij}\omega_{jk}=\delta^{i}_{k}, and can be used to raise indices. This is the matter content summarized in our last work [36].




Occasionally, it will prove convenient for our holographic calculations to keep the trace part of  X^{\dot{\alpha}}_{ij} Xi​jα˙X^{\dot{\alpha}}_{ij} explicitly in the chiral algebra, rather than only taking the trace-free exterior square. This is analogous to the choice to work with  \mathrm{U}(N) U⁡(N)\mathrm{U}(N) rather than  \mathrm{SU}(N) SU⁡(N)\mathrm{SU}(N) gauge group in 4d  \mathcal{N}=4 𝒩=4\mathcal{N}=4 super Yang-Mills. It corresponds to the center-of-mass degrees of freedom in the bulk, or dually the  E[1,0],E[0,1] E⁡[1,0],E⁡[0,1]E[1,0],E[0,1] (see the definition (4.17)) generators in the chiral algebra, which can be consistently removed. We will find it convenient to keep the center of mass modes nonvanishing when building our holographic dictionary in section 8, and use them in section 9.3 to test the gravitational sector of our duality.




The matter fields  I_{ri} Ir​iI_{ri} describe D1-D5 strings. The index  r rr in  I_{ri} Ir​iI_{ri} is an  \mathrm{SO}(8) SO⁡(8)\mathrm{SO}(8) index for a basis of  \mathbb{C}^{8} ℂ8\mathbb{C}^{8}. The Lagrangian for the free theory is





 \int\omega^{ij}\delta^{rs}I_{ri}\bar{\partial}I_{sj}+\int\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\omega^{ik}\omega^{jl}X^{\dot{\alpha}}_{ij}\bar{\partial}X^{\dot{\beta}}_{kl}. ∫ωi​j​δr​s​Ir​i​∂¯​Is​j+∫ϵα˙​β˙​ωi​k​ωj​l​Xi​jα˙​∂¯​Xk​lβ˙.\int\omega^{ij}\delta^{rs}I_{ri}\bar{\partial}I_{sj}+\int\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\omega^{ik}\omega^{jl}X^{\dot{\alpha}}_{ij}\bar{\partial}X^{\dot{\beta}}_{kl}.

(4.3)


This leads to the OPE (where we have absorbed some factors of  2\pi\mathrm{i} 2​π​i2\pi\mathrm{i} into rescaling the fields)

(4.4)

This means we introduce  \mathrm{b}-\mathrm{c} b−c\mathrm{b}-\mathrm{c} ghosts transforming in the adjoint representation of  \mathrm{Sp}(N) Sp⁡(N)\mathrm{Sp}(N).
