Appendix A More on curved twistor spaces




A.1 Twistors for self-dual spacetimes




Let  M MM be an oriented 4-manifold equipped with a Riemannian metric  g gg. Take  \theta^{a} θa\theta^{a},  a=0,1,2,3 a=0,1,2,3a=0,1,2,3, to be a local frame of the cotangent bundle of  M MM satisfying  g=\delta_{ab}\theta^{a}\theta^{b} g=δa​b​θa​θbg=\delta_{ab}\theta^{a}\theta^{b}. We define its spinor equivalent  \theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} θα​α˙\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}},  \alpha=1,2 α=1,2\alpha=1,2,  \dot{\alpha}=\dot{1},\dot{2} α˙=1˙,2˙\dot{\alpha}=\dot{1},\dot{2}, as follows





 \theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\theta^{0}+\mathrm{i}\,\theta^{3}&&\theta^{2}+\mathrm{i}\,\theta^{1}\\
-\theta^{2}+\mathrm{i}\,\theta^{1}&&\theta^{0}-\mathrm{i}\,\theta^{3}\end{pmatrix}\,. θα​α˙=12​(θ0+i​θ3θ2+i​θ1−θ2+i​θ1θ0−i​θ3).\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\theta^{0}+\mathrm{i}\,\theta^{3}&&\theta^{2}+\mathrm{i}\,\theta^{1}\\
-\theta^{2}+\mathrm{i}\,\theta^{1}&&\theta^{0}-\mathrm{i}\,\theta^{3}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(A.1)


In terms of  \theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} θα​α˙\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}, the metric can be expressed as





 g=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\,\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\,\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}} g=ϵα​β​ϵα˙​β˙​θα​α˙​θβ​β˙g=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\,\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\,\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}

(A.2)


where  \epsilon_{\alpha\beta} ϵα​β\epsilon_{\alpha\beta},  \epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} ϵα˙​β˙\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} are  2\times 2 2×22\times 2 Levi-Civita symbols. Since  \theta^{a} θa\theta^{a} are real, we observe that





 \theta^{2\dot{1}}=-\overline{\theta^{1\dot{2}}}\,,\qquad\theta^{2\dot{2}}=\overline{\theta^{1\dot{1}}}\,. θ2​1˙=−θ1​2˙¯,θ2​2˙=θ1​1˙¯.\theta^{2\dot{1}}=-\overline{\theta^{1\dot{2}}}\,,\qquad\theta^{2\dot{2}}=\overline{\theta^{1\dot{1}}}\,.

(A.3)


The dual frame of vector fields will be denoted  e_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} eα​α˙e_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}, so that  e_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\,\lrcorner\,\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}=\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}\delta_{\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{\beta}} eα​α˙​⌟​θβ​β˙=δαβ​δα˙β˙e_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\,\lrcorner\,\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}=\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}\delta_{\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{\beta}}. Let  \Lambda^{-} Λ−\Lambda^{-} and  \Lambda^{+} Λ+\Lambda^{+} be the rank 3 bundles of 2-forms on  M MM that are respectively ASD or SD with respect to  g gg. Denote by  \Sigma^{\alpha\beta}\equiv\Sigma^{(\alpha\beta)} Σα​β≡Σ(α​β)\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}\equiv\Sigma^{(\alpha\beta)} and  \tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\equiv\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})} Σ~α˙​β˙≡Σ~(α˙​β˙)\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\equiv\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})} the following choice of local frames for  \Lambda^{-} Λ−\Lambda^{-} and  \Lambda^{+} Λ+\Lambda^{+} respectively:





 \Sigma^{\alpha\beta}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\,,\qquad\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\,. Σα​β=ϵα​β​θα​α˙∧θβ​β˙,Σ~α˙​β˙=ϵα˙​β˙​θα​α˙∧θβ​β˙.\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\,,\qquad\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\,.

(A.4)


This choice is standard in the literature [78]. These 2-forms obey the reality conditions  \overline{\Sigma^{12}}=-\Sigma^{12} Σ12¯=−Σ12\overline{\Sigma^{12}}=-\Sigma^{12},  \overline{\Sigma^{22}}=\Sigma^{11} Σ22¯=Σ11\overline{\Sigma^{22}}=\Sigma^{11}, etc.




The Levi-Civita connection  \Gamma_{\alpha\beta}\equiv\Gamma_{(\alpha\beta)} Γα​β≡Γ(α​β)\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}\equiv\Gamma_{(\alpha\beta)} on  \Lambda^{-} Λ−\Lambda^{-} obeys the structure equation





 \mathrm{d}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}=2\,\Gamma^{(\alpha}{}_{\gamma}\wedge\Sigma^{\beta)\gamma}\,, dΣα​β=2Γ(αCLOSEγ∧ΣOPENβ)​γ,\mathrm{d}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}=2\,\Gamma^{(\alpha}{}_{\gamma}\wedge\Sigma^{\beta)\gamma}\,,

(A.5)


where spinor indices are raised or lowered using the conventions (2.4). It is called the ASD spin connection. Rotations of the frame  \Sigma^{\alpha\beta} Σα​β\Sigma^{\alpha\beta} induce  \mathrm{SU}(2) SU⁡(2)\mathrm{SU}(2) gauge transformations on  \Gamma_{\alpha\beta} Γα​β\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}. The curvature of the ASD spin connection is given by





 R_{\alpha\beta}=\mathrm{d}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}+\Gamma_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}\wedge\Gamma_{\gamma\beta}\,. Rα​β=dΓα​β+Γαγ∧Γγ​β.R_{\alpha\beta}=\mathrm{d}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}+\Gamma_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}\wedge\Gamma_{\gamma\beta}\,.

(A.6)


This computes the ASD part of the Riemann tensor 2-form. It admits a well-known decomposition into irreps of  \mathrm{Spin}(4)\simeq\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{SU}(2) Spin⁡(4)≃SU⁡(2)×SU⁡(2)\mathrm{Spin}(4)\simeq\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{SU}(2):





 R_{\alpha\beta}=\Psi_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\Sigma^{\gamma\delta}+\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}+\frac{R}{12}\,\Sigma_{\alpha\beta} Rα​β=Ψα​β​γ​δ​Σγ​δ+Φα​β​α˙​β˙​Σ~α˙​β˙+R12​Σα​βR_{\alpha\beta}=\Psi_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\Sigma^{\gamma\delta}+\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}+\frac{R}{12}\,\Sigma_{\alpha\beta}

(A.7)


where  \Psi_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta} Ψα​β​γ​δ\Psi_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta} is the spinor equivalent of the ASD Weyl curvature,  \Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} Φα​β​α˙​β˙\Phi_{\alpha\beta\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} the trace-free Ricci tensor, and  R RR the Ricci scalar.




Similarly, one can also define the SD spin connection  \tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} Γ~α˙​β˙\tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} via the structure equation  \mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=2\,\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\gamma}}\wedge\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\beta})\dot{\gamma}} dΣ~α˙​β˙=2Γ~(α˙CLOSEγ˙∧Σ~OPENβ˙)​γ˙\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}=2\,\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\gamma}}\wedge\tilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{\beta})\dot{\gamma}}, and its curvature tensors are defined analogously. Putting  \Gamma_{\alpha\beta} Γα​β\Gamma_{\alpha\beta} and  \tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} Γ~α˙​β˙\tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}} together, one obtains the full Levi-Civita connection  \Gamma_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta\dot{\beta}}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}+\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta} Γα​α˙​β​β˙=ϵα​β​Γ~α˙​β˙+ϵα˙​β˙​Γα​β\Gamma_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta\dot{\beta}}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}+\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}. This fits into Cartan’s structure equation for the coframe,  \mathrm{d}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=-\Gamma^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\wedge\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}} dθα​α˙=−Γα​α˙β​β˙∧θβ​β˙\mathrm{d}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=-\Gamma^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\wedge\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}. We will work chirally and mainly use the ASD spin connection for the construction of twistor spaces.




Twistor space  Z ZZ can be defined as the projective bundle of undotted (left-handed) Weyl spinors on  M MM. This is well-defined even if  M MM is not spin, because spinor representations of  \mathrm{Spin}(4) Spin⁡(4)\mathrm{Spin}(4) descend to well-defined projective representations of  \mathrm{SO}(4) SO⁡(4)\mathrm{SO}(4). Alternatively, it is diffeomorphic to the sphere bundle  S(\Lambda^{-}) S⁡(Λ−)S(\Lambda^{-}) of the bundle of ASD 2-forms on  M MM.




Let  \sigma_{\alpha} σα\sigma_{\alpha} be homogeneous coordinates on the  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} fibers of the projection  \pi:Z\to M π:Z→M\pi:Z\to M. At a given  x\in M x∈Mx\in M, a point  \sigma_{\alpha} σα\sigma_{\alpha} on its twistor line  L_{x}=\pi^{-1}(\{x\}) Lx=π−1​({x})L_{x}=\pi^{-1}(\{x\}) labels the almost complex structure on  T^{*}_{x}M\otimes\mathbb{C} Tx∗​M⊗ℂT^{*}_{x}M\otimes\mathbb{C} whose  (1,0) (1,0)(1,0)-forms are spanned by  \sigma_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} σα​θα​α˙\sigma_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}. It is straightforward to verify that this is metric and orientation compatible. Also introduce the quaternionic conjugates  \hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}=(-\overline{\sigma_{2}},\overline{\sigma_{1}}) σ^α=(−σ2¯,σ1¯)\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}=(-\overline{\sigma_{2}},\overline{\sigma_{1}}) that transform in the same  \mathrm{SU}(2) SU⁡(2)\mathrm{SU}(2) representation as  \sigma_{\alpha} σα\sigma_{\alpha}. They satisfy the useful property  \langle\hat{\sigma}\,\sigma\rangle=\|\sigma\|^{2}\equiv|\sigma_{1}|^{2}+|\sigma_{2}|^{2} ⟨σ^​σ⟩=‖σ‖2≡|σ1|2+|σ2|2\langle\hat{\sigma}\,\sigma\rangle=\|\sigma\|^{2}\equiv|\sigma_{1}|^{2}+|\sigma_{2}|^{2}. The map  \sigma_{\alpha}\mapsto\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha} σα↦σ^α\sigma_{\alpha}\mapsto\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha} is just the familiar antipodal map at the level of the  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} fibers. It lifts to  Z ZZ as an involution without fixed points, thereby inducing a real structure on  Z ZZ.




Now, whereas horizontal forms on  Z ZZ are canonically defined to be the span of pullbacks of  \theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} θα​α˙\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}, we need a connection on  Z ZZ to define vertical 1-forms. This is provided by the ASD spin connection, in terms of which we take





 \nabla\sigma^{\alpha}=\mathrm{d}\sigma^{\alpha}-\Gamma^{\alpha}{}_{\beta}\sigma^{\beta}\,,\qquad\nabla\hat{\sigma}^{\alpha}=\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma}^{\alpha}-\Gamma^{\alpha}{}_{\beta}\hat{\sigma}^{\beta} ∇σα=d​σα−Γα​σββ,∇σ^α=d​σ^α−Γα​σ^ββ\nabla\sigma^{\alpha}=\mathrm{d}\sigma^{\alpha}-\Gamma^{\alpha}{}_{\beta}\sigma^{\beta}\,,\qquad\nabla\hat{\sigma}^{\alpha}=\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma}^{\alpha}-\Gamma^{\alpha}{}_{\beta}\hat{\sigma}^{\beta}

(A.8)


as a frame for the bundle of vertical 1-forms. The reality conditions on  \Gamma^{\alpha}{}_{\beta} Γαβ\Gamma^{\alpha}{}_{\beta} resulting from those on  \Sigma^{\alpha\beta} Σα​β\Sigma^{\alpha\beta} ensure that  \nabla\hat{\sigma}^{\alpha}=(\widehat{\nabla\sigma})^{\alpha} ∇σ^α=(∇σ^)α\nabla\hat{\sigma}^{\alpha}=(\widehat{\nabla\sigma})^{\alpha}. The Atiyah-Hitchin-Singer almost complex structure is defined by declaring





 \tau=\sigma_{\alpha}\nabla\sigma^{\alpha}\,,\qquad\theta^{\dot{\alpha}}=\sigma_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} τ=σα∇σα,θα˙=σαθα​α˙\tau=\sigma_{\alpha}\nabla\sigma^{\alpha}\,,\qquad\theta^{\dot{\alpha}}=\sigma_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}

(A.9)


to be a local basis of  (1,0) (1,0)(1,0)-forms  \Omega^{1,0}(Z) Ω1,0​(Z)\Omega^{1,0}(Z) and





 \bar{\tau}=\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}\nabla\hat{\sigma}^{\alpha}}{\|\sigma\|^{4}}\,,\qquad\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}=-\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}}{\|\sigma\|^{2}} τ¯=σ^α∇σ^α‖σ‖4,θ¯α˙=−σ^α​θα​α˙‖σ‖2\bar{\tau}=\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}\nabla\hat{\sigma}^{\alpha}}{\|\sigma\|^{4}}\,,\qquad\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}=-\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}}{\|\sigma\|^{2}}

(A.10)


as a standard local basis of the complex conjugate  (0,1) (0,1)(0,1)-forms  \Omega^{0,1}(Z) Ω0,1​(Z)\Omega^{0,1}(Z). They are designed to have zero scaling weight in  \hat{\sigma}_{\alpha} σ^α\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}. In this almost complex structure, the canonical bundle  K_{Z}=\Omega^{3,0}(Z) KZ=Ω3,0​(Z)K_{Z}=\Omega^{3,0}(Z) admits the local frame





 \tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}}\,. τ∧θ1˙∧θ2˙.\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}}\,.

(A.11)


This has scaling weight  +4 +4+4 in the homogeneous coordinates  \sigma_{\alpha} σα\sigma_{\alpha}. As a result, local sections of  K_{Z} KZK_{Z} are given by  \varphi\,\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}} φ​τ∧θ1˙∧θ2˙\varphi\,\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}} for smooth functions  \varphi(x,\sigma,\hat{\sigma}) φ⁡(x,σ,σ^)\varphi(x,\sigma,\hat{\sigma}) of weight  -4 −4-4 in  \sigma_{\alpha} σα\sigma_{\alpha} and  0 00 in  \hat{\sigma}_{\alpha} σ^α\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}. In particular,  K_{Z} KZK_{Z} restricts to the bundle  \mathscr{O}(-4)\to\mathbb{CP}^{1} 𝒪⁡(−4)→ℂ​ℙ1\mathscr{O}(-4)\to\mathbb{CP}^{1} on every twistor line.




Let  \pi_{p,q} πp,q\pi_{p,q} denote the projection of a  (p+q) (p+q)(p+q)-form onto its  (p,q) (p,q)(p,q) part. Starting with the exterior derivative  \mathrm{d} d\mathrm{d} on  Z ZZ, the holomorphic and antiholomorphic exterior derivatives of smooth forms  \alpha\in\Omega^{p,q}(Z) α∈Ωp,q​(Z)\alpha\in\Omega^{p,q}(Z) are defined to be the projections  \partial\alpha\vcentcolon=\pi_{p+1,q}(\mathrm{d}\alpha) ∂α:=πp+1,q​(d​α)\partial\alpha\vcentcolon=\pi_{p+1,q}(\mathrm{d}\alpha),  \bar{\partial}\alpha\vcentcolon=\pi_{p,q+1}(\mathrm{d}\alpha) ∂¯​α:=πp,q+1​(d​α)\bar{\partial}\alpha\vcentcolon=\pi_{p,q+1}(\mathrm{d}\alpha). The almost complex structure is said to be integrable if  \mathrm{d}=\partial+\bar{\partial} d=∂+∂¯\mathrm{d}=\partial+\bar{\partial}. This happens if and only if  \tau,\theta^{\dot{\alpha}} τ,θα˙\tau,\theta^{\dot{\alpha}} span a closed differential ideal, or equivalently when the kernel of  \tau,\theta^{\dot{\alpha}} τ,θα˙\tau,\theta^{\dot{\alpha}} forms an involutive distribution of vector fields. It is a textbook calculation to verify that





 \displaystyle\mathrm{d}\theta^{\dot{\alpha}} d​θα˙\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\theta^{\dot{\alpha}}
 \displaystyle=\tau\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\tilde{\Gamma}^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{\beta}}+\frac{\langle\hat{\sigma}\,\nabla\sigma\rangle}{\|\sigma\|^{2}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{\alpha}} =τ∧θ¯α˙+Γ~α˙β˙∧θβ˙+⟨σ^∇σ⟩‖σ‖2∧θα˙\displaystyle=\tau\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\tilde{\Gamma}^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{\beta}}+\frac{\langle\hat{\sigma}\,\nabla\sigma\rangle}{\|\sigma\|^{2}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{\alpha}}





 \displaystyle\equiv 0\quad\text{mod span}\{\tau,\theta^{\dot{\alpha}}\}\,, ≡0mod span{τ,θα˙},\displaystyle\equiv 0\quad\text{mod span}\{\tau,\theta^{\dot{\alpha}}\}\,,

(A.12)



 \displaystyle\mathrm{d}\tau d​τ\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\tau
 \displaystyle=\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}R_{\alpha\beta}+2\,\frac{\langle\hat{\sigma}\,\nabla\sigma\rangle}{\|\sigma\|^{2}}\wedge\tau =σα​σβ​Rα​β+2​⟨σ^∇σ⟩‖σ‖2∧τ\displaystyle=\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}R_{\alpha\beta}+2\,\frac{\langle\hat{\sigma}\,\nabla\sigma\rangle}{\|\sigma\|^{2}}\wedge\tau





 \displaystyle\equiv\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}\sigma^{\gamma}\sigma^{\delta}\Psi_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\,\bar{\theta}_{\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}\quad\text{mod span}\{\tau,\theta^{\dot{\alpha}}\}\,. ≡σασβσγσδΨα​β​γ​δθ¯α˙∧θ¯α˙mod span{τ,θα˙}.\displaystyle\equiv\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}\sigma^{\gamma}\sigma^{\delta}\Psi_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\,\bar{\theta}_{\dot{\alpha}}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}\quad\text{mod span}\{\tau,\theta^{\dot{\alpha}}\}\,.

(A.13)


Hence, the Atiyah-Hitchin-Singer almost complex structure is integrable if and only if the ASD Weyl curvature vanishes [46, 47], i.e.,  (M,g) (M,g)(M,g) is self-dual. See [45, 126, 101] for helpful reviews.





A.2 Applications to scalar-flat Kähler geometry




Next, let  g gg be scalar-flat Kähler, or equivalently self-dual and Kähler. The associated Kähler form  \omega ω\omega gives a classic example of a globally defined, nowhere vanishing ASD 2-form (in our conventions). With the choice of frame (A.4) for  \Lambda^{-} Λ−\Lambda^{-}, it can be expanded as





 \omega=\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{\alpha\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}\,. ω=i​ωα​β​Σα​β.\omega=\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{\alpha\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}\,.

(A.14)


In our orientation, this should satisfy  \frac{1}{2!}\,\omega^{2}=-\mathrm{vol}_{g} 12!​ω2=−volg\frac{1}{2!}\,\omega^{2}=-\mathrm{vol}_{g}. Using  \Sigma^{\alpha\beta}\wedge\Sigma^{\gamma\delta}=4\epsilon^{\alpha(\gamma}\epsilon^{\delta)\beta}\mathrm{vol}_{g} Σα​β∧Σγ​δ=4​ϵα⁡(γCLOSE​ϵOPENδ)​β​volg\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}\wedge\Sigma^{\gamma\delta}=4\epsilon^{\alpha(\gamma}\epsilon^{\delta)\beta}\mathrm{vol}_{g}, this constrains the determinant of the coefficient matrix  \omega_{\alpha\beta} ωα​β\omega_{\alpha\beta} to be





 \det\omega_{\alpha\beta}=\omega_{11}\omega_{22}-\omega_{12}^{2}=-\frac{1}{4}\,. detωα​β=ω11​ω22−ω122=−14.\det\omega_{\alpha\beta}=\omega_{11}\omega_{22}-\omega_{12}^{2}=-\frac{1}{4}\,.

(A.15)


Demanding  \mathrm{d}\omega=0 d​ω=0\mathrm{d}\omega=0 on  M MM yields the twistor equation (see lemma 1.1 in [37])





 \nabla_{(\gamma|\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{|\alpha\beta)}=0\,, ∇(γ|γ˙CLOSEω|αβ)=0,\nabla_{(\gamma|\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{|\alpha\beta)}=0\,,

(A.16)


where  \nabla_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{\alpha\beta}=e_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{\alpha\beta}+2\,\omega_{\delta(\alpha}\Gamma^{\delta}{}_{\beta)\gamma\dot{\gamma}} ∇γ​γ˙ωα​β=eγ​γ˙ωα​β+2ωδ⁡(αCLOSEΓδOPENβ)​γ​γ˙\nabla_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{\alpha\beta}=e_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{\alpha\beta}+2\,\omega_{\delta(\alpha}\Gamma^{\delta}{}_{\beta)\gamma\dot{\gamma}} is the covariant derivative. We can pull back  \omega_{\alpha\beta} ωα​β\omega_{\alpha\beta} to  Z ZZ and, following [37], use it to define a section of the bundle  K_{Z}^{-1/2} KZ−1/2K_{Z}^{-1/2},





 \check{\omega}=\omega_{\alpha\beta}\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}\,. ωˇ=ωα​β​σα​σβ.\check{\omega}=\omega_{\alpha\beta}\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}\,.

(A.17)


As expected, this is a section of  \mathscr{O}(2) 𝒪⁡(2)\mathscr{O}(2) on every twistor line  L_{x} LxL_{x}. Computing its antiholomorphic exterior derivative in the complex structure of  Z ZZ yields





 \bar{\partial}\check{\omega}=\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}\,\pi_{0,1}(\nabla\omega_{\alpha\beta})=\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}\sigma^{\gamma}\nabla_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{\alpha\beta}\,\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\gamma}}=0\,, ∂¯​ωˇ=σα​σβ​π0,1​(∇ωα​β)=σα​σβ​σγ​∇γ​γ˙ωα​β​θ¯γ˙=0,\bar{\partial}\check{\omega}=\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}\,\pi_{0,1}(\nabla\omega_{\alpha\beta})=\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}\sigma^{\gamma}\nabla_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{\alpha\beta}\,\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\gamma}}=0\,,

(A.18)


where  \nabla\omega_{\alpha\beta}\equiv\theta^{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\,\nabla_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{\alpha\beta} ∇ωα​β≡θγ​γ˙​∇γ​γ˙ωα​β\nabla\omega_{\alpha\beta}\equiv\theta^{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\,\nabla_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\omega_{\alpha\beta}. In this way, the Kähler form on  M MM lifts to a holomorphic global section of  K_{Z}^{-1/2} KZ−1/2K_{Z}^{-1/2}. Due to the reality condition  \overline{\omega}=\omega ω¯=ω\overline{\omega}=\omega, this section is pure imaginary under the real structure of  Z ZZ, i.e., it satisfies  \overline{\check{\omega}(x,\sigma)}=-\omega(x,\hat{\sigma}) ωˇ​(x,σ)¯=−ω⁡(x,σ^)\overline{\check{\omega}(x,\sigma)}=-\omega(x,\hat{\sigma}).




Since  \check{\omega} ωˇ\check{\omega} and  \tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}} τ∧θ1˙∧θ2˙\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}} are both holomorphic, we conclude that scalar-flat Kähler metrics give rise to a meromorphic 3-form on their twistor spaces:





 \Omega=\frac{\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}}}{\check{\omega}^{2}}\,. Ω=τ∧θ1˙∧θ2˙ωˇ2.\Omega=\frac{\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}}}{\check{\omega}^{2}}\,.

(A.19)


This is manifestly invariant under scalings of  \sigma_{\alpha} σα\sigma_{\alpha}, so is projectively well-defined. Since  \check{\omega} ωˇ\check{\omega} is a global section,  \Omega Ω\Omega is globally defined on  Z-\{\check{\omega}=0\} Z−{ωˇ=0}Z-\{\check{\omega}=0\}. Its only singularities are double poles at the locations of the zeroes of  \check{\omega} ωˇ\check{\omega}. Let us verify that we can recover the Kähler form (A.14) from this  3 33-form by contour integrating around one of these poles as in (2.1). Introduce affine coordinates  \sigma_{\alpha}=(1,\sigma) σα=(1,σ)\sigma_{\alpha}=(1,\sigma). At fixed  x\in M x∈Mx\in M, we can factorize





 \check{\omega}=\omega_{11}\sigma^{2}-2\omega_{12}\sigma+\omega_{22}=\omega_{11}(\sigma-\sigma_{+})(\sigma-\sigma_{-}) ωˇ=ω11​σ2−2​ω12​σ+ω22=ω11​(σ−σ+)​(σ−σ−)\check{\omega}=\omega_{11}\sigma^{2}-2\omega_{12}\sigma+\omega_{22}=\omega_{11}(\sigma-\sigma_{+})(\sigma-\sigma_{-})

(A.20)


in terms of  x xx-dependent roots  \sigma_{\pm}(x) σ±​(x)\sigma_{\pm}(x) given by





 \sigma_{\pm}=\frac{\omega_{12}\pm\sqrt{\omega_{12}^{2}-\omega_{11}\omega_{22}}}{\omega_{11}}\,. σ±=ω12±ω122−ω11​ω22ω11.\sigma_{\pm}=\frac{\omega_{12}\pm\sqrt{\omega_{12}^{2}-\omega_{11}\omega_{22}}}{\omega_{11}}\,.

(A.21)


Similarly, we can write  \theta^{\dot{\alpha}}=\theta^{1\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}} θα˙=θ1​α˙+σ​θ2​α˙\theta^{\dot{\alpha}}=\theta^{1\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}} and  \tau=\mathrm{d}\sigma+\Gamma_{11}\sigma^{2}-2\Gamma_{12}\sigma+\Gamma_{22} τ=d​σ+Γ11​σ2−2​Γ12​σ+Γ22\tau=\mathrm{d}\sigma+\Gamma_{11}\sigma^{2}-2\Gamma_{12}\sigma+\Gamma_{22}. Performing a contour integral around the pole  \sigma=\sigma_{-} σ=σ−\sigma=\sigma_{-}, we obtain





 \frac{1}{2\pi}\oint_{|\sigma-\sigma_{-}|=\varepsilon}\Omega=\frac{\mathrm{i}\,[\Sigma^{11}+(\sigma_{+}+\sigma_{-})\Sigma^{12}+\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\Sigma^{22}]}{\omega_{11}^{2}(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})^{3}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{\alpha\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}}{(-4\det\omega_{\alpha\beta})^{3/2}}\,. 12​π​∮|σ−σ−|=εΩ=i⁡[Σ11+(σ++σ−)​Σ12+σ+​σ−​Σ22]ω112​(σ+−σ−)3=i​ωα​β​Σα​β(−4detωα​β)3/2.\frac{1}{2\pi}\oint_{|\sigma-\sigma_{-}|=\varepsilon}\Omega=\frac{\mathrm{i}\,[\Sigma^{11}+(\sigma_{+}+\sigma_{-})\Sigma^{12}+\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\Sigma^{22}]}{\omega_{11}^{2}(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})^{3}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{\alpha\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta}}{(-4\det\omega_{\alpha\beta})^{3/2}}\,.

(A.22)


Remembering that  \det\omega_{\alpha\beta}=-\frac{1}{4} detωα​β=−14\det\omega_{\alpha\beta}=-\frac{1}{4}, this reduces precisely to (A.14). This also fixes the overall normalization  \omega_{11} ω11\omega_{11} to be  \omega_{11}=(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})^{-1} ω11=(σ+−σ−)−1\omega_{11}=(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})^{-1}.




Geometrically, the roots  \sigma_{\pm}(x) σ±​(x)\sigma_{\pm}(x) define almost complex structures on  M MM with  (1,0) (1,0)(1,0)-forms spanned by  \theta^{1\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma_{\pm}\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}} θ1​α˙+σ±​θ2​α˙\theta^{1\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma_{\pm}\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}}. The reality condition  \overline{\check{\omega}(x,\sigma)}=-\check{\omega}(x,\hat{\sigma}) ωˇ​(x,σ)¯=−ωˇ​(x,σ^)\overline{\check{\omega}(x,\sigma)}=-\check{\omega}(x,\hat{\sigma}) requires that  \sigma_{+}=-1/\overline{\sigma_{-}} σ+=−1/σ−¯\sigma_{+}=-1/\overline{\sigma_{-}}, i.e.,  \sigma_{+} σ+\sigma_{+} is antipodal to  \sigma_{-} σ−\sigma_{-}. So the two almost complex structures are conjugate to each other. As a result,





 \omega=\frac{\mathrm{i}\,\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,(\theta^{1\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma_{-}\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}})\wedge(\theta^{1\dot{\beta}}+\sigma_{+}\theta^{2\dot{\beta}})}{(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})} ω=i​ϵα˙​β˙​(θ1​α˙+σ−​θ2​α˙)∧(θ1​β˙+σ+​θ2​β˙)(σ+−σ−)\omega=\frac{\mathrm{i}\,\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,(\theta^{1\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma_{-}\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}})\wedge(\theta^{1\dot{\beta}}+\sigma_{+}\theta^{2\dot{\beta}})}{(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})}

(A.23)


is of type  (1,1) (1,1)(1,1) in either of these almost complex structures. Without loss of generality, we can then identify  \sigma_{-} σ−\sigma_{-} with the complex structure with respect to which  \omega ω\omega is the Kähler form on  M MM. The associated  (0,1) (0,1)(0,1)-vector fields on  M MM are spanned by  \sigma_{-}e_{1\dot{\alpha}}-e_{2\dot{\alpha}} σ−​e1​α˙−e2​α˙\sigma_{-}e_{1\dot{\alpha}}-e_{2\dot{\alpha}} and define the antiholomorphic exterior derivative of smooth functions  f ff on  M MM,





 \bar{\partial}f=\frac{(\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}}-\overline{\sigma_{-}}\theta^{1\dot{\alpha}})}{1+|\sigma_{-}|^{2}}\,(e_{2\dot{\alpha}}-\sigma_{-}e_{1\dot{\alpha}})f\,. ∂¯​f=(θ2​α˙−σ−¯​θ1​α˙)1+|σ−|2​(e2​α˙−σ−​e1​α˙)​f.\bar{\partial}f=\frac{(\theta^{2\dot{\alpha}}-\overline{\sigma_{-}}\theta^{1\dot{\alpha}})}{1+|\sigma_{-}|^{2}}\,(e_{2\dot{\alpha}}-\sigma_{-}e_{1\dot{\alpha}})f\,.

(A.24)


The holomorphic exterior derivative  \partial ∂\partial on  M MM is defined by complex conjugation.




The construction of  \check{\omega} ωˇ\check{\omega} from  \omega ω\omega is a special case of the Penrose transform relating solutions of the twistor equation (A.16) to elements of  H^{0}(Z,K_{Z}^{-1/2}) H0(Z,KZ−1/2)H^{0}(Z,K_{Z}^{-1/2}) [100]. In particular, the converse of this correspondence also holds [56]: any holomorphic global section  \check{\omega}\in H^{0}(Z,K_{Z}^{-1/2}) ωˇ∈H0(Z,KZ−1/2)\check{\omega}\in H^{0}(Z,K_{Z}^{-1/2}) vanishing at exactly two distinct points on every  L_{x} LxL_{x} and satisfying  \overline{\check{\omega}(x,\sigma)}=-\omega(x,\hat{\sigma}) ωˇ​(x,σ)¯=−ω⁡(x,σ^)\overline{\check{\omega}(x,\sigma)}=-\omega(x,\hat{\sigma}) gives rise to a Kähler metric on  M MM that is conformal to its self-dual metric  g gg.




Indeed, since  \check{\omega}|_{L_{x}} ωˇ|Lx\check{\omega}|_{L_{x}} is a global section of  \mathscr{O}(2)\to\mathbb{CP}^{1} 𝒪⁡(2)→ℂ​ℙ1\mathscr{O}(2)\to\mathbb{CP}^{1}, one can use Liouville’s theorem to conclude that it is a degree 2 polynomial in  \sigma σ\sigma of the form  \check{\omega}|_{L_{x}}=\omega_{\alpha\beta}(x)\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta} ωˇ|Lx=ωα​β​(x)​σα​σβ\check{\omega}|_{L_{x}}=\omega_{\alpha\beta}(x)\sigma^{\alpha}\sigma^{\beta}. Let  \sigma_{\alpha}=(1,\sigma_{\pm}(x)) σα=(1,σ±​(x))\sigma_{\alpha}=(1,\sigma_{\pm}(x)) be the roots of  \check{\omega}|_{L_{x}}=0 ωˇ|Lx=0\check{\omega}|_{L_{x}}=0, and use them to define a normalized global section of the projective spinor bundle of  M MM,





 \psi_{\alpha}=\frac{(1,\sigma_{-})}{\sqrt{1+|\sigma_{-}|^{2}}}\,,\qquad\langle\hat{\psi}\,\psi\rangle=\|\psi\|^{2}=1\,. ψα=(1,σ−)1+|σ−|2,⟨ψ^​ψ⟩=‖ψ‖2=1.\psi_{\alpha}=\frac{(1,\sigma_{-})}{\sqrt{1+|\sigma_{-}|^{2}}}\,,\qquad\langle\hat{\psi}\,\psi\rangle=\|\psi\|^{2}=1\,.

(A.25)


The reality condition  \sigma_{+}=-1/\overline{\sigma_{-}} σ+=−1/σ−¯\sigma_{+}=-1/\overline{\sigma_{-}} allows us to express the coefficients  \omega_{\alpha\beta} ωα​β\omega_{\alpha\beta} as





 \omega_{\alpha\beta}=\varphi\psi_{(\alpha}\hat{\psi}_{\beta)} ωα​β=φ​ψ(αCLOSE​ψ^OPENβ)\omega_{\alpha\beta}=\varphi\psi_{(\alpha}\hat{\psi}_{\beta)}

(A.26)


where  \varphi(x) φ⁡(x)\varphi(x) is a real and nowhere vanishing normalization factor that will play the role of conformal scale.




As seen via (A.18), the holomorphicity of  \check{\omega} ωˇ\check{\omega} is equivalent to the twistor equation (A.16) for  \omega_{\alpha\beta} ωα​β\omega_{\alpha\beta}. Contracting the latter with  \psi^{\alpha}\psi^{\beta}\psi^{\gamma} ψα​ψβ​ψγ\psi^{\alpha}\psi^{\beta}\psi^{\gamma} yields





 \psi^{\alpha}\psi^{\beta}\psi^{\gamma}\nabla_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\omega_{\beta\gamma}=0\implies\psi^{\alpha}\psi^{\beta}\nabla_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\psi_{\beta}=0\,. ψα​ψβ​ψγ​∇α​α˙ωβ​γ=0⟹ψα​ψβ​∇α​α˙ψβ=0.\psi^{\alpha}\psi^{\beta}\psi^{\gamma}\nabla_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\omega_{\beta\gamma}=0\implies\psi^{\alpha}\psi^{\beta}\nabla_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\psi_{\beta}=0\,.

(A.27)


At the same time, a short calculation shows that





 \begin{split}\mathrm{d}(\psi_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}})&=\nabla_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\psi_{\alpha}\,\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\wedge\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+\tilde{\Gamma}^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\wedge\psi_{\beta}\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\\
&\equiv\psi^{\alpha}\psi^{\beta}\nabla_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\psi_{\alpha}\,\hat{\psi}_{\delta}\theta^{\delta\dot{\beta}}\wedge\hat{\psi}_{\gamma}\theta^{\gamma\dot{\alpha}}\quad\text{mod span}\{\psi_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\}\,.\end{split} d⁡(ψα​θα​α˙)=∇β​β˙ψαθβ​β˙∧θα​α˙+Γ~α˙β˙∧ψβθβ​β˙≡ψαψβ∇β​β˙ψαψ^δθδ​β˙∧ψ^γθγ​α˙mod span{ψαθα​α˙}.\begin{split}\mathrm{d}(\psi_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}})&=\nabla_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\psi_{\alpha}\,\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\wedge\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+\tilde{\Gamma}^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\wedge\psi_{\beta}\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\\
&\equiv\psi^{\alpha}\psi^{\beta}\nabla_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\psi_{\alpha}\,\hat{\psi}_{\delta}\theta^{\delta\dot{\beta}}\wedge\hat{\psi}_{\gamma}\theta^{\gamma\dot{\alpha}}\quad\text{mod span}\{\psi_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\}\,.\end{split}

(A.28)


Hence, (A.27) is sufficient to ensure that the 1-forms  \psi_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} ψα​θα​α˙\psi_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} span a closed differential ideal and give rise to a complex structure on  M MM. The spacetime 2-form  \omega=\mathrm{i}|\varphi|\psi_{\alpha}\hat{\psi}_{\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta} ω=i​|φ|​ψα​ψ^β​Σα​β\omega=\mathrm{i}|\varphi|\psi_{\alpha}\hat{\psi}_{\beta}\Sigma^{\alpha\beta} is then closed by virtue of (A.16), positive, and is of type  (1,1) (1,1)(1,1) in this complex structure, so it yields a Kähler form on  M MM. The associated Kähler metric is





 \begin{split}2\,\omega_{\alpha\beta}\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}&=2|\varphi|\cdot\psi_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\cdot\hat{\psi}_{\beta}\theta^{\beta\dot{\alpha}}\\
&=|\varphi|\,\langle\hat{\psi}\,\psi\rangle\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\theta_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=|\varphi|\,g\,,\end{split} 2​ωα​β​ϵα˙​β˙​θα​α˙​θβ​β˙=2|φ|⋅ψαθαα˙⋅ψ^βθβ​α˙=|φ|⟨ψ^​ψ⟩​θα​α˙​θα​α˙=|φ|​g,\begin{split}2\,\omega_{\alpha\beta}\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\theta^{\beta\dot{\beta}}&=2|\varphi|\cdot\psi_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\cdot\hat{\psi}_{\beta}\theta^{\beta\dot{\alpha}}\\
&=|\varphi|\,\langle\hat{\psi}\,\psi\rangle\,\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\theta_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=|\varphi|\,g\,,\end{split}

(A.29)


which is conformal to the SD metric  g gg that we originally used to construct twistor space. Choosing the conformal scale to be  \varphi=1 φ=1\varphi=1 is then equivalent to normalizing the Kähler form to satisfy  \omega^{2}=-2\,\mathrm{vol}_{g} ω2=−2​volg\omega^{2}=-2\,\mathrm{vol}_{g}. Lastly, scalar-flatness follows from self-duality of  g gg, as proven for instance in [50].






Appendix B Reducing twistor actions to spacetime




In sections 2.2 and 2.3, we mentioned certain holomorphic theories on twistor space  Z ZZ and the 4-dimensional integrable field theories that they give rise to on a scalar-flat Kähler spacetime  (M,g) (M,g)(M,g). In this appendix, we provide more details on reducing our twistor actions to spacetime via compactification along the  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} fibers of the twistor fibration  \pi:Z\to M π:Z→M\pi:Z\to M. In performing such reductions, we will use the local presentation of twistor spaces described in appendix A.




B.1 Holomorphic Chern-Simons theory




Let  E\to Z E→ZE\to Z be a complex vector bundle with structure group  G GG, and let  \mathcal{A} 𝒜\mathcal{A} be a partial connection on it as in section 2.2. We start by studying the holomorphic Chern-Simons action given in (2.18). Its reduction to spacetime will be performed by generalizing the methods of [127] to twistor actions on curved self-dual backgrounds.




The partial connection  \mathcal{A} 𝒜\mathcal{A} can be decomposed into vertical and horizontal  (0,1) (0,1)(0,1)-forms on  Z ZZ in the basis (A.10):





 \mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}_{0}\bar{\tau}+\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,. 𝒜=𝒜0​τ¯+𝒜α˙​θ¯α˙.\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}_{0}\bar{\tau}+\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}\,.

(B.1)


The vertical part  \mathcal{A}_{0}\bar{\tau} 𝒜0​τ¯\mathcal{A}_{0}\bar{\tau} acts as a partial connection on the restriction of  E EE to the twistor lines  L_{x} LxL_{x}.  \mathcal{A} 𝒜\mathcal{A} is weightless in the homogeneous coordinates  \sigma_{\alpha} σα\sigma_{\alpha}, so  \mathcal{A}_{0} 𝒜0\mathcal{A}_{0} must have weight  +2 +2+2 and  \mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}} 𝒜α˙\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}} must have weight  +1 +1+1. Similarly, all of these have weight  0 00 in the complex conjugates  \hat{\sigma}_{\alpha} σ^α\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}. A short computation using  \Psi_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}=R=0 Ψα​β​γ​δ=R=0\Psi_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}=R=0 establishes that





 \bar{\partial}\mathcal{A}=\left(\bar{\partial}_{0}\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}}-\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}_{0}\right)\bar{\tau}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma^{\alpha}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\alpha}{}^{\dot{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}}\right)\bar{\theta}_{\dot{\gamma}}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\gamma}}\,, ∂¯​𝒜=(∂¯0​𝒜α˙−∂¯α˙​𝒜0)​τ¯∧θ¯α˙+12​(∂¯α˙​𝒜α˙+σα​Γ~α˙​β˙​α​𝒜α˙β˙)​θ¯γ˙∧θ¯γ˙,\bar{\partial}\mathcal{A}=\left(\bar{\partial}_{0}\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}}-\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}_{0}\right)\bar{\tau}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma^{\alpha}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\alpha}{}^{\dot{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}}\right)\bar{\theta}_{\dot{\gamma}}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\gamma}}\,,

(B.2)


written in terms of a basis of  (0,1) (0,1)(0,1)-vector fields on  Z ZZ,





 \bar{\partial}_{0}=-\|\sigma\|^{2}\,\sigma_{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}}\,,\qquad\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}=\sigma^{\alpha}\bigg(e_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma^{\beta}\Gamma_{\beta\gamma\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{\gamma}}+\hat{\sigma}^{\beta}\Gamma_{\beta\gamma\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{\sigma}_{\gamma}}\bigg)\,. ∂¯0=−‖σ‖2​σα​∂∂σ^α,∂¯α˙=σα​(eα​α˙+σβ​Γβ​γ​α​α˙​∂∂σγ+σ^β​Γβ​γ​α​α˙​∂∂σ^γ).\bar{\partial}_{0}=-\|\sigma\|^{2}\,\sigma_{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}}\,,\qquad\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}=\sigma^{\alpha}\bigg(e_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma^{\beta}\Gamma_{\beta\gamma\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{\gamma}}+\hat{\sigma}^{\beta}\Gamma_{\beta\gamma\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{\sigma}_{\gamma}}\bigg)\,.

(B.3)


This can be further simplified by noticing that  \sigma^{\alpha}e_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma^{\alpha}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\alpha}{}^{\dot{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}}=\sigma^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}} σα​eα​α˙​𝒜α˙+σα​Γ~α˙​β˙​α​𝒜α˙β˙=σα​∇α​α˙𝒜α˙\sigma^{\alpha}e_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\sigma^{\alpha}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\alpha}{}^{\dot{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}}=\sigma^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}^{\dot{\alpha}}, where  \nabla_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} ∇α​α˙\nabla_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} is the Levi-Civita connection of  g gg. This is useful when performing manipulations involving integration-by-parts in actions like (2.18).




Plugging (B.2) in the action (2.18) shows that  \mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}} 𝒜α˙\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}} occurs quadratically. Integrating out  \mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}} 𝒜α˙\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}} is then equivalent to imposing its equation of motion, which is seen to be





 \bar{\partial}_{0}\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}}-\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}_{0}+[\mathcal{A}_{0},\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}}]=0\,. ∂¯0​𝒜α˙−∂¯α˙​𝒜0+[𝒜0,𝒜α˙]=0.\bar{\partial}_{0}\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}}-\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{A}_{0}+[\mathcal{A}_{0},\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}}]=0\,.

(B.4)


This is the gauge constraint associated to the vertical part of the gauge symmetry (2.21). To eliminate it, we start by defining a frame  H\in\text{Maps}(Z,G) H∈Maps​(Z,G)H\in\text{Maps}(Z,G) on the vector bundle  E\to Z E→ZE\to Z that trivializes the partial connection  \mathcal{A} 𝒜\mathcal{A} on every twistor line,





 \mathcal{A}_{0}=H^{-1}\bar{\partial}_{0}H\,. 𝒜0=H−1​∂¯0​H.\mathcal{A}_{0}=H^{-1}\bar{\partial}_{0}H\,.

(B.5)


This is possible because  E EE was assumed to be trivial along the twistor lines. Note that  H HH has homogeneity  0 00 in  \sigma_{\alpha},\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha} σα,σ^α\sigma_{\alpha},\hat{\sigma}_{\alpha}. In this frame, (B.4) allows us to show that





 \bar{\partial}_{0}\!\left(H(\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}+\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}})H^{-1}\right)=H[\bar{\partial}_{0},\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}]H^{-1}=0\,, ∂¯0​(H⁡(∂¯α˙+𝒜α˙)​H−1)=H⁡[∂¯0,∂¯α˙]​H−1=0,\bar{\partial}_{0}\!\left(H(\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}+\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}})H^{-1}\right)=H[\bar{\partial}_{0},\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}]H^{-1}=0\,,

(B.6)


having used the fact  [\bar{\partial}_{0},\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}]=0 [∂¯0,∂¯α˙]=0[\bar{\partial}_{0},\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}]=0 that can be verified by direct computation by remembering that  \Gamma_{\alpha\beta\gamma\dot{\gamma}}=\Gamma_{\beta\alpha\gamma\dot{\gamma}} Γα​β​γ​γ˙=Γβ​α​γ​γ˙\Gamma_{\alpha\beta\gamma\dot{\gamma}}=\Gamma_{\beta\alpha\gamma\dot{\gamma}}. Hence,  H(\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}+\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}})H^{-1} H⁡(∂¯α˙+𝒜α˙)​H−1H(\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}+\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}})H^{-1} are a pair of weight  1 11, globally holomorphic functions on each  \mathbb{CP}^{1} ℂ​ℙ1\mathbb{CP}^{1} fiber. By Liouville’s theorem, they must be linear in  \sigma^{\alpha} σα\sigma^{\alpha}, so that





 H(\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}+\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}})H^{-1}=\sigma^{\alpha}A_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} H⁡(∂¯α˙+𝒜α˙)​H−1=σα​Aα​α˙H(\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}+\mathcal{A}_{\dot{\alpha}})H^{-1}=\sigma^{\alpha}A_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}

(B.7)


for some Lie algebra valued covector  A_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} Aα​α˙A_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} on  M MM.




Putting (B.5) and (B.7) together, we obtain the partial solution





 \mathcal{A}=H^{-1}(\bar{\partial}+\pi_{0,1}A)H 𝒜=H−1​(∂¯+π0,1​A)​H\mathcal{A}=H^{-1}(\bar{\partial}+\pi_{0,1}A)H

(B.8)


of the equations of motion of holomorphic Chern-Simons theory. Here,





 \pi_{0,1}A=\sigma^{\alpha}A_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}} π0,1​A=σα​Aα​α˙​θ¯α˙\pi_{0,1}A=\sigma^{\alpha}A_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}

(B.9)


is the  (0,1) (0,1)(0,1) part of the pullback of  A=A_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} A=Aα​α˙​θα​α˙A=A_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\theta^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}} to  Z ZZ, and





 \bar{\partial}H=\bar{\partial}_{0}H\,\bar{\tau}+\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}H\,\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}} ∂¯​H=∂¯0​H​τ¯+∂¯α˙​H​θ¯α˙\bar{\partial}H=\bar{\partial}_{0}H\,\bar{\tau}+\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}H\,\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}

(B.10)


can be obtained from the definition of  \bar{\partial} ∂¯\bar{\partial}. To physically interpret  A AA, one constructs a vector bundle  E_{M}\to M EM→ME_{M}\to M from the bundle  E\to Z E→ZE\to Z satisfying  \pi^{*}E_{M}=E π∗​EM=E\pi^{*}E_{M}=E.3939
              39
              
              
              
            Again, such a bundle exists because  E EE is trivial along the fibers of  \pi:Z\to M π:Z→M\pi:Z\to M. The fiber of  E_{M} EME_{M} over  x\in M x∈Mx\in M is taken to be the vector space of sections  \Gamma(L_{x},E) Γ⁡(Lx,E)\Gamma(L_{x},E) that are holomorphic with respect to  (\bar{\partial}+a)|_{L_{x}} (∂¯+a)|Lx(\bar{\partial}+a)|_{L_{x}}. Then  A AA provides a connection on  E_{M} EME_{M}.




The boundary conditions (2.19) on  \mathcal{A} 𝒜\mathcal{A} can be used to further constrain  A AA. To do this, let us specialize to affine coordinates  \sigma_{\alpha}=(1,\sigma) σα=(1,σ)\sigma_{\alpha}=(1,\sigma). In (A.20), we denoted the zeroes of  \check{\omega} ωˇ\check{\omega} by  \sigma=\sigma_{\pm} σ=σ±\sigma=\sigma_{\pm}. We gauge fix  \mathcal{A} 𝒜\mathcal{A} by imposing the conditions [38, 39]

• 



 H HH depends on  \sigma σ\sigma only through  |\sigma| |σ||\sigma| and is independent of arg (\sigma) (σ)(\sigma),

• 



 H=\mathbbm{1} H=𝟙H=\mathbbm{1} in a small neighborhood of  \sigma=\sigma_{+} σ=σ+\sigma=\sigma_{+} on each  L_{x} LxL_{x},

• 



 H=\mathsf{g} H=𝗀H=\mathsf{g} in a small neighborhood of  \sigma=\sigma_{-} σ=σ−\sigma=\sigma_{-} on each  L_{x} LxL_{x},



where  \mathsf{g}(x) 𝗀⁡(x)\mathsf{g}(x) is a Lie algebra valued function on  M MM containing the dynamical degree of freedom in our theory. Evaluating (B.8) at  \sigma=\sigma_{\pm} σ=σ±\sigma=\sigma_{\pm} in this gauge, and using the boundary conditions  \mathcal{A}|_{\sigma=\sigma_{\pm}}=0 𝒜|σ=σ±=0\mathcal{A}|_{\sigma=\sigma_{\pm}}=0, we can solve for the spacetime gauge field in terms of  \mathsf{g} 𝗀\mathsf{g} to find





 A=-\bar{\partial}\mathsf{g}\,\mathsf{g}^{-1}\,, A=−∂¯​𝗀​𝗀−1,A=-\bar{\partial}\mathsf{g}\,\mathsf{g}^{-1}\,,

(B.11)


where the spacetime antiholomorphic derivative  \bar{\partial}\mathsf{g} ∂¯​𝗀\bar{\partial}\mathsf{g} is defined as in (A.24) by setting  f=\mathsf{g} f=𝗀f=\mathsf{g}.




Substituting (B.8) in the holomorphic Chern-Simons action (2.18) and using the fact that  \pi_{0,1}A π0,1​A\pi_{0,1}A is purely horizontal, we are left with





 S_{\text{hCS}}[\mathcal{A}]=\frac{1}{2(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{Z}\Omega\wedge\bar{\partial}\,\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\bar{\partial}H\,H^{-1}\wedge\pi_{0,1}A\right)-\frac{1}{6(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{Z}\Omega\wedge\mathrm{tr}\!\left(H^{-1}\bar{\partial}H\right)^{3}\,. ShCS​[𝒜]=12​(2​π​i)3​∫ZΩ∧∂¯​tr​(∂¯​H​H−1∧π0,1​A)−16​(2​π​i)3​∫ZΩ∧tr​(H−1​∂¯​H)3.S_{\text{hCS}}[\mathcal{A}]=\frac{1}{2(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{Z}\Omega\wedge\bar{\partial}\,\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\bar{\partial}H\,H^{-1}\wedge\pi_{0,1}A\right)-\frac{1}{6(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{Z}\Omega\wedge\mathrm{tr}\!\left(H^{-1}\bar{\partial}H\right)^{3}\,.

(B.12)


Integrating the  \bar{\partial} ∂¯\bar{\partial} in the first term by parts generates a factor of





 \bar{\partial}\Omega=-2\pi\mathrm{i}\,(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})^{2}\left\{\frac{\partial_{\sigma}\delta(\sigma-\sigma_{-})}{(\sigma-\sigma_{+})^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{\sigma}\delta(\sigma-\sigma_{+})}{(\sigma-\sigma_{-})^{2}}\right\}(1+|\sigma|^{2})^{2}\,\bar{\tau}\wedge\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}}\,, ∂¯​Ω=−2​π​i​(σ+−σ−)2​{∂σδ⁡(σ−σ−)(σ−σ+)2+∂σδ⁡(σ−σ+)(σ−σ−)2}​(1+|σ|2)2​τ¯∧τ∧θ1˙∧θ2˙,\bar{\partial}\Omega=-2\pi\mathrm{i}\,(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})^{2}\left\{\frac{\partial_{\sigma}\delta(\sigma-\sigma_{-})}{(\sigma-\sigma_{+})^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{\sigma}\delta(\sigma-\sigma_{+})}{(\sigma-\sigma_{-})^{2}}\right\}(1+|\sigma|^{2})^{2}\,\bar{\tau}\wedge\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}}\,,

(B.13)


having used  \bar{\partial}_{0}=(1+|\sigma|^{2})^{2}\,\partial_{\bar{\sigma}} ∂¯0=(1+|σ|2)2∂σ¯\bar{\partial}_{0}=(1+|\sigma|^{2})^{2}\,\partial_{\bar{\sigma}}, the identity  \partial_{\bar{\sigma}}(\sigma-z)^{-1}=2\pi\mathrm{i}\,\delta(\sigma-z) ∂σ¯(σ−z)−1=2​π​i​δ​(σ−z)\partial_{\bar{\sigma}}(\sigma-z)^{-1}=2\pi\mathrm{i}\,\delta(\sigma-z), and the normalization condition  \omega_{11}(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})=\sqrt{-4\det\omega_{\alpha\beta}}=1 ω11​(σ+−σ−)=−4detωα​β=1\omega_{11}(\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-})=\sqrt{-4\det\omega_{\alpha\beta}}=1 encountered in appendix A. Using this to simplify the first term of (B.12) after integration by parts, we see that the contribution localized at  \sigma=\sigma_{+} σ=σ+\sigma=\sigma_{+} vanishes in the aforementioned gauge. We are left with the contribution localized at  \sigma=\sigma_{-} σ=σ−\sigma=\sigma_{-}, which quickly reduces to






 \displaystyle\frac{1}{2(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{Z}\bar{\partial}\Omega\wedge\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\bar{\partial}H\,H^{-1}\wedge\pi_{0,1}A\right) 12​(2​π​i)3​∫Z∂¯​Ω∧tr⁡(∂¯​H​H−1∧π0,1​A)\displaystyle\frac{1}{2(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{Z}\bar{\partial}\Omega\wedge\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\bar{\partial}H\,H^{-1}\wedge\pi_{0,1}A\right)





 \displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{vol}_{g}}{\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-}}\,\mathrm{tr}\left\{\mathsf{g}^{-1}(e_{2\dot{\alpha}}\mathsf{g}-\sigma_{+}e_{1\dot{\alpha}}\mathsf{g})\,\mathsf{g}^{-1}(e_{2\dot{\alpha}}\mathsf{g}-\sigma_{-}e_{1\dot{\alpha}}\mathsf{g})\right\} =−18​π2∫volgσ+−σ−tr{𝗀−1(e2​α˙𝗀−σ+e1​α˙𝗀)𝗀−1(e2​α˙𝗀−σ−e1​α˙𝗀)}\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{vol}_{g}}{\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{-}}\,\mathrm{tr}\left\{\mathsf{g}^{-1}(e_{2\dot{\alpha}}\mathsf{g}-\sigma_{+}e_{1\dot{\alpha}}\mathsf{g})\,\mathsf{g}^{-1}(e_{2\dot{\alpha}}\mathsf{g}-\sigma_{-}e_{1\dot{\alpha}}\mathsf{g})\right\}

(B.14)


where we have used  (1+|\sigma|^{2})^{2}\,\bar{\tau}\wedge\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{1}}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{2}}=-\mathrm{d}\bar{\sigma}\wedge\mathrm{d}\sigma\wedge\mathrm{vol}_{g} (1+|σ|2)2​τ¯∧τ∧θ1˙∧θ2˙∧θ¯1˙∧θ¯2˙=−d​σ¯∧d​σ∧volg(1+|\sigma|^{2})^{2}\,\bar{\tau}\wedge\tau\wedge\theta^{\dot{1}}\wedge\theta^{\dot{2}}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{1}}\wedge\bar{\theta}^{\dot{2}}=-\mathrm{d}\bar{\sigma}\wedge\mathrm{d}\sigma\wedge\mathrm{vol}_{g} for performing the  \sigma σ\sigma integral. In going from the second to the third line, we have used the identity  \mathrm{vol}_{g}=-\frac{1}{2}\,\omega^{2} volg=−12​ω2\mathrm{vol}_{g}=-\frac{1}{2}\,\omega^{2}, the expression (A.23) for the Kähler form, and the definition (A.24) for the spacetime antiholomorphic derivative  \bar{\partial}\mathsf{g} ∂¯​𝗀\bar{\partial}\mathsf{g} (and its complex conjugate  \partial\mathsf{g} ∂𝗀\partial\mathsf{g}).




The second term in (B.12) reduces to a five-dimensional Wess-Zumino term. Firstly, because  \Omega Ω\Omega is a  (3,0) (3,0)(3,0)-form, we can trivially replace  H^{-1}\bar{\partial}H H−1​∂¯​HH^{-1}\bar{\partial}H by the de Rham differential  H^{-1}\mathrm{d}H H−1​d​HH^{-1}\mathrm{d}H on twistor space. Since  H HH is gauge fixed to be independent of the phase of  \sigma σ\sigma, the integral over  \sigma σ\sigma now factorizes into a 5d integral times a contour integral of  \Omega Ω\Omega over  \arg(\sigma)\in S^{1} arg⁡(σ)∈S1\arg(\sigma)\in S^{1},





 -\frac{1}{6(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{Z}\Omega\wedge\mathrm{tr}\!\left(H^{-1}\mathrm{d}H\right)^{3}=\frac{-\mathrm{i}}{48\pi^{3}}\int_{M\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\bigg(\oint_{S^{1}}\Omega\bigg)\wedge\mathrm{tr}(H^{-1}\mathrm{d}H)^{3}\,. −16​(2​π​i)3∫ZΩ∧tr(H−1dH)3=−i48​π3∫M×ℝ+(∮S1Ω)∧tr(H−1dH)3.-\frac{1}{6(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{Z}\Omega\wedge\mathrm{tr}\!\left(H^{-1}\mathrm{d}H\right)^{3}=\frac{-\mathrm{i}}{48\pi^{3}}\int_{M\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\bigg(\oint_{S^{1}}\Omega\bigg)\wedge\mathrm{tr}(H^{-1}\mathrm{d}H)^{3}\,.

(B.15)


The contour integral extracts the residue at one of the two poles of  \Omega Ω\Omega. Recalling (2.1), this reproduces  -2\pi\omega −2​π​ω-2\pi\omega (the sign arises from a choice of orientation).
